**Chosen:** Claims are preassigned from `heard` alone, and claimed clusters are excluded from `resolve_with` entirely. The bulk path skips `stale_exemplars` and its rebuild; `Reseed` is a three-way state so a bulk Meeting with no plan cannot fall through as an ordinary run.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **Overriding after the fact let a claim steal a seed.** `resolve_with`'s mutual-best asks each seed which cluster it likes best, and a claimed cluster left in that comparison can answer for a seed it will never be given: a cluster the record already calls Alice whose centroid is an exact match for Bob's seed made an otherwise clean Bob fragment fail mutual-best and come back `New`, after which the claimed cluster was reassigned to Alice regardless. Nobody was recognized as Bob and nothing in the answer said why. Pinned by `a_claimed_cluster_does_not_take_another_speakers_seed` with Bob's fragment at 0.900 against his own seed and 0.436 against Alice's — clear of floor and margin, so only the claim can break it. **The override also sat behind `embeddings.get`,** so a claim was dropped whenever its cluster had no embedding — which `live::assemble` omits when a centroid is unavailable while still keeping the turns. A claim assigns words; it neither needs a vector nor licenses one. **The legacy rebuild is the wrong evidence for a re-run.** `runner::rebuild` re-cuts a window around each stale exemplar's saved offsets — the previous model's guess at where a voice was — which is precisely what a re-run is throwing away. Running it beside the bounded plan would embed the same Speakers twice from two notions of where they spoke, with the legacy one adopting first. `Reseed::Gone` is defensive against the race where the recording is deleted between `get_meeting` and `plan`; the same hazard at the commit end is reachable and covered.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 188181b

## Q220 — diarization/03 — deviation

**Question:** Does the same-window counter say how many forbidden pairs the unconstrained run would actually have merged?
**Options considered:** keep Q216's "most were never a merge the constraint had to prevent" / withdraw it as unmeasured
**Chosen:** Withdrawn. The counts are forbidden pairs counted **whether or not the unconstrained run merged them**; what share of them it would have merged was never measured.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** `same_window_merges` counts a forbidden rejoin from the pair map and the reference labels alone — it never asks the unconstrained clustering whether that pair came out merged. "Most were never a merge the constraint had to prevent" is a plausible reading of why the ratio is invalid, but it is a quantitative claim the counter does not support, and putting it in the reason a ratio was withdrawn repeats the error being corrected. The invalidity stands on what *was* measured: the two counts have different denominators and different questions behind them. No new measurement; the same six cells, read for what they say.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q216 — only its "most were never a merge" clause; every other correction it records stands.
**Ref:** 188181b

## Q221 — diarization/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Where does the bulk re-run's startup trigger live, and what keeps it from firing on a History that never asked for one?
**Options considered:** a caller that checks the schema before asking / the gate inside `begin_if_the_model_changed`, with the caller unconditional
**Chosen:** The gate moved into `begin_if_the_model_changed`, which now answers `Ok(None)` on a History without the re-run tables instead of failing. `Core::rerun_if_the_model_changed` is awaited in `start_daemon` (`lib.rs:61`, the call at `lib.rs:89`) before the never-diarized catch-up, and swallows errors into a warning.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **This is the one function of the module a Core calls on every start**, including every installation in the field, where the tables are not in `MIGRATIONS`. The rest of `store::rerun` is allowed to fail there because nothing reaches it without a backlog already existing; this is reached unconditionally, so the gate belongs beside `is_bulk_work`'s rather than in a caller that has to remember it. Pinned by `asking_at_startup_whether_the_model_changed_is_safe_without_the_tables`, which asserts the *answer* — a caller that swallows an `Err` into a startup warning cannot tell the two apart, so "nothing was enqueued" would have passed either way. Nothing is recorded on a bare History either: there is nowhere to keep it, and inventing a row is the half-installed state `installed` exists to refuse. **Awaited rather than spawned**, unlike the catch-up beside it: on every History today it is one `sqlite_master` lookup, and where it does enqueue, its oldest-first order has to be in the queue before the catch-up adds what a previous Core left. Neither starts a model. **Still inert**: the trigger exists and is unreachable, `PENDING_MODEL_CHANGE_RERUN` and `PENDING_MODEL_CHANGE_WIPE` stay out of `MIGRATIONS`, and the schema tests that assert their absence are untouched. Registration and any real wipe remain the user's.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 8c744d5

## Q222 — diarization/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** How are "resume rather than restart" and "cancel reports honestly how far it got" checked without models or real audio?
**Options considered:** wait for a real corpus run / drive the real completion path with synthetic runs and compare whole end states
**Chosen:** Offline, through `finish_run` over a synthetic run, comparing an interrupted backlog's final state against an uninterrupted one field for field in a single assertion.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Both acceptance criteria are about the queue and the re-run's arithmetic, and both live in the record — no model is involved in either. A walk is `finish_run`, the same completion path a real pass reaches, so the queue row still leaves inside the attribution transaction. **Resume** builds the same backlog twice, walks one Meeting and drops the Core in the second, restarts on the same History, runs the real trigger, and asserts the remaining queue, the re-run block and the evidence counts all equal the uninterrupted run's; a trigger that compared anything but the stored identity re-enqueues the walked Meeting, which the mutation confirms fails it. **Cancel** walks one of three, stops the backlog, and asserts `(3, 1, 0, 2, true)` through `diarize_status` — the Client's own view — then restarts and asserts the stop is not resurrected. `done` is the number that can lie, since cancelled Meetings also leave the queue; dropping the `abandoned` subtraction fails that test. What this does **not** establish is that a real model's vectors recognize the same people after a re-run, which is measurement and still waits on the model decision.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 8c744d5

## Q223 — diarization/05 — tradeoff

**Question:** Should the three upgrade-path tests keep addressing `MIGRATIONS` by bare index, when appending the pending wipe would not move them?
**Options considered:** leave them and check after registration, as the ticket deferred / name every migration now and derive each index from the migration it means
**Chosen:** All fourteen migrations are named constants, `MIGRATIONS` is a list of those names, and the three tests call a `before(migration)` helper instead of writing `11`, `10` and `9`.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** I judged the work marginal and said so; the advisor loop selected it anyway, and I did it in full. No person chose — per the advisor the direction began as Claude Code's own ghost-text prompt suggestion, which the advisor agent accepted and submitted as its skill tells it to — so this is `agent`, which this journal distinguishes from a decision a person made. The reasoning I gave still describes the cost and the benefit honestly. **Cost:** a 236-line diff over `schema.rs` for a hazard that registration alone could not trigger, since appending never moves a prefix. **Benefit:** each test now says which upgrade it stands in front of rather than which position, so a migration inserted anywhere but the end leaves them testing what they were written to test. That was demonstrated rather than asserted — inserting a migration at position 0 left all three upgrade-path tests passing and unchanged, where a bare `MIGRATIONS[..9]` would have applied a different set while still reading as a prune test. `every_migration_is_distinct_so_naming_one_is_unambiguous` guards the assumption `before` rests on and pins the order, so a deliberate reordering has to be looked at by a person rather than absorbed silently. **Every one of the fourteen SQL bodies is byte-identical to before**, verified by extracting both and comparing; only the wrapper around each changed, so no History sees a different migration. Ticket 05's open criterion is now met ahead of the registration it was deferred to. Registration itself is untouched: `PENDING_MODEL_CHANGE_WIPE` and `PENDING_MODEL_CHANGE_RERUN` stay out of `MIGRATIONS` and both tests asserting that still pass.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** bca0d69

## Q224 — diarization/12 — deviation

**Question:** Two earlier entries stated something untrue — one named a function that does not exist, the other credited a person who did not decide. Correct them in place, or append a superseding entry?
**Options considered:** append, per `log-decisions`' append-only rule / edit Q221 and Q223 where they stand
**Chosen:** Edited in place, on direction. Q221's `lib.rs::run` now reads `start_daemon` (`lib.rs:61`, call at `lib.rs:89`). Q223's `Decided-by` now reads `agent`, and its "overruled" sentence now says what happened. This entry is the record that both were wrong and were changed.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **The two corrections.** There is no `run` in that module; `grep` puts the only call to `rerun_if_the_model_changed` at `lib.rs:89`, and the module's public entry points are `start_daemon` and `run_daemon`. I wrote a plausible name rather than the one in the file. And `Decided-by: human` claimed an authority nobody exercised: the direction arrived as a turn in the advisor loop, which per the advisor began as an accepted ghost-text prompt suggestion. Either way no person chose, and this journal keeps `human` for decisions a person made even when an agent surfaced the options. **The method.** `AGENTS.md`'s `log-decisions` makes this file append-only and sanctions in-place change only for renumbering, so I first appended a superseding entry and said I would edit instead if that was wanted. It was, and this is that edit. The rule's purpose — that a reader can see what the record used to say — is served by this entry rather than by the untouched text, which is why it is rewritten to describe the corrections rather than deleted along with them. The engineering content of Q221 and Q223 is unchanged and still accurate. No code changed by any of this.
**Outcome:** applied

**Ref:** 555b650

## Q225 — interactive/land-the-rework-branch — deviation

**Question:** The user asked to land `diarization-pyannote-redimnet2` on `main` and to work on `main` only from here on. What does "land" mean for a branch whose every ticket already reached `main` by another route?
**Options considered:** `git merge` and resolve the conflicts / archive the branch as a tag, bring across only what `main` lacks, delete the branch / leave the branch as it is
**Chosen:** Archive and extract. The branch is the annotated tag `archive/diarization-pyannote-redimnet2` at its old tip, `scripts/export-redimnet2.py` and `crates/evertranscript-core/examples/redimnet_spike.rs` are on `main`, the two NOTES.md citations that told a reader to go and read the branch now name the tag, and the branch is deleted. Nothing else of it comes across.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** A dry-run merge (`git merge-tree --write-tree`) conflicts in 24 paths, all in the diarization core, and three of them cannot be resolved by choosing hunks: the branch's `schema.rs` has the Voiceprint wipe and `diarize_rerun` **inside** `MIGRATIONS`, where `main` holds both as unregistered `PENDING_*` on the user's instruction, so any textual merge of that array registers the wipe on every History in the field; the two `DECISIONS.md` files forked at Q115 and both claim Q115–Q124 for different decisions, and this journal's numbering is an invariant; and the branch deletes `fbank.rs`, which `main`'s `live.rs` imports, because it ships ReDimNet2-B3 as the embedding where `main` ships WeSpeaker — the ticket-06 decision that is still the user's. Per-commit audit against `main`'s tip: each of the twenty commits has exactly one file still differing, its `DECISIONS.md`, so nothing of substance was left behind; NOTES.md records the route each ticket took. The two files brought across are the only branch-only artifacts `main` had a use for — the earlier journal entry that rebuilt the model offline cites the script by path and says it survives on the branch, which a deleted branch would have made false. Not brought: the twelve original ticket files (NOTES.md says why — they describe a starting state that no longer exists), a `DiarizeRerunStatus.ts` binding `main`'s generator did not emit, and the branch's older ADR-0037, which `main` has as a superset. The tag rather than plain deletion because NOTES.md tells a reader to read Q135 and Q145 there, and a name that survives `gc` is what makes that citation resolve. `-D` on the branch is what the plan called for since it is unmerged by design.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** f5ff970 and the following commit

## Q226 — diarization/06 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Which embedding ships — WeSpeaker ResNet34-LM, what is in the build, or ReDimNet2-B3, the model this workstream was measuring against it?
**Options considered:** keep WeSpeaker / adopt ReDimNet2-B3 / adopt ReDimNet2-B3 and register the model-change wipe and re-run with it
**Chosen:** ReDimNet2-B3, now; the migrations later. The registry entry is the change: `redimnet2-b3-vox2-lm`, our own export published unchanged, stamped `redimnet2-b3` version `1`, the identity the harness has stamped its ReDimNet2 vectors with throughout. `Frontend` moved from a loader argument onto `VoiceprintId`, so the one place that names the model names its input, and `Embedder::embed` — the entry the bounded re-seed and the exemplar rebuild use — dispatches on it rather than computing fbank unconditionally, which was an `ort` input error for this model and only ever ran for the other. The waveform path's floor is spelled as the fbank path's, `FRAME_LENGTH + 8 · FRAME_SHIFT` samples, so the two refuse the same audio. The harness defaults to the shipped model and keeps WeSpeaker measurable under `diarize-embedding-wespeaker.onnx` with its old stamp spelled out.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user chose, on the measured record and after being told what it does and does not establish: unconstrained DER 26.01% against 29.13% on dev and 24.62% against 28.64% held-out (Q140, Q143), the held-out gap all confusion; recognition dominant on all four quantities at the declared primary points (Q153); against that, the same-window constraint closing the gap to 0.43 and reversing its sign (Q149) — a substitution, not a refutation, and one with a measured recognition cost. The ground ADR-0037 first gave, the 0% EER, stays withdrawn (Q115); this adoption rests on the DER table. "Register the migrations later" keeps the wipe and re-run out of `MIGRATIONS`, which means an installed History carries WeSpeaker Voiceprints under the old stamp until then; `resolve` refuses them by name, so the cost of the interval is every returning Speaker read as a stranger, not a mis-score. Verified against the real export through the production loader: loads, 192 wide, L2-normalized, and the WeSpeaker file under the same loader now fails with `Invalid input name: waveform` — the mismatch is an error, not a plausible vector of the wrong thing. Same `filename` as before on purpose: an installed WeSpeaker file reads as `Corrupted` on size and is fetched afresh, and the published artifact's `x-linked-size` and `x-linked-etag` match the registry byte for byte.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q139 — which model ships was escalated there and is answered here.
**Ref:** f9ff073

## Q227 — diarization/06 — deviation

**Question:** Q226 and the ADR-0037 amendment of the same day say that until the wipe is registered an installed History's WeSpeaker Voiceprints are "refused by name" and "every returning Speaker is a stranger". Is that what the code does?
**Options considered:** the claim stands / the claim is wrong and the record is corrected / the claim is wrong and the lazy path is closed before registration
**Chosen:** The claim is wrong; the record is corrected and no behaviour changes. On the ordinary path — `Reseed::Ordinary`, every Diarization on every History in the field — `server.rs` reads `stale_exemplars(EMBEDDING_MODEL, EMBEDDING_MODEL_VERSION)`, which after the adoption selects every WeSpeaker exemplar in the store; `runner::rebuild` reads each one's saved sample window from the kept audio and embeds it with the loaded model; and `adopt_rebuilt`, first thing inside the attribution transaction and gated on nothing, files the ReDimNet2 vector under `redimnet2-b3`/`1`, deletes any row with no window or no Meeting, and recomputes every touched Speaker's Voiceprint. The seeds `resolve` then reads are those. So the first ordinary Diarization after upgrade moves the whole store, not one Meeting's share, and a returning Speaker is recognized — on evidence of the kind ADR-0037's third paragraph rejected: WeSpeaker's choice of where each voice was, re-embedded, rather than the Operator's whole attributed clusters. Q226's `Justification`, the amendment and the registry entry's doc are corrected to say so; the amendment and the doc in place, being a day old and the worker's own, Q226 by this entry.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Raised by the advisor from NOTES.md's own account of the lazy path (its "What `main` does today" and "05's migration" paragraphs), verified against `server.rs` (`let stale = if bulk { Vec::new() } else { stale_exemplars(…) }`, `adopt_rebuilt` at the head of the write transaction), `runner::rebuild` and `cluster::adopt_rebuilt`; the `evidence_from_an_old_front_end_is_rebuilt_before_it_is_compared` test exercises exactly this sequence through its seams. NOTES.md was right and Q226 was wrong: "refused by name" describes `cosine`'s and `seeds`' guard against comparing across spaces, and that guard is never reached because the rows are moved into the new space before anything is compared. **What the interval before registration actually costs:** not recognition — it costs the *kind* of evidence. Every Voiceprint on every installed History will be ReDimNet2 vectors of WeSpeaker's cuts until the wipe discards them and the re-run rebuilds them from attribution; a Speaker whose exemplars have no window or whose Meeting is gone loses its Voiceprint in the interval rather than at the wipe; and the wipe, when it lands, throws away work the interval did. Nothing has measured how much worse WeSpeaker's cuts are than attributed clusters under ReDimNet2. **Recommendation, not action:** register soon rather than later, because the lazy path's work is discarded anyway and each day widens the population of Histories that will see two disruptions instead of one; and if the interval is to be long, decide whether to close the lazy path for a model change (leave it for a front-end version bump, which is what it was built for) — a policy choice, the user's.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q226 — its `Justification`'s account of the interval; the adoption itself and everything else in it stands.
**Ref:** 50325be

## Q228 — diarization/05+12 — irreversible-action

**Question:** Register the model-change wipe and re-run in `MIGRATIONS`? Every History in the field then loses every Voiceprint and exemplar at its next launch and has all of History walked again; the row cannot be un-wiped.
**Options considered:** append both, as written / append both and seed the re-run row with the identity the wipe emptied / keep holding
**Chosen:** Both appended, in that order and last — `MODEL_CHANGE_WIPE` is migration 15 and `MODEL_CHANGE_RERUN` is 16, renamed from `PENDING_*` since they no longer are — and the re-run body ends with one `INSERT`: `diarize_rerun` row 1 with `wespeaker-voxceleb-resnet34-LM`/`2`, total 0, cancelled 0, abandoned 0. The wipe's SQL is byte-identical to what Q223 verified. Registration alone was a wipe without a re-run: `begin_if_the_model_changed` treats an absent row as a first start and records the current identity without walking anything (Q221's guard, which is right and stays), and a History the wipe has just emptied, with the table created empty a moment later, is exactly that — no walk, and nothing left for the lazy path to rebuild. The seed is the one fact the migration knows, the space every vector it removed was stamped with; at total 0 `Rerun::requested()` is false, so no Client is shown a backlog in the instant before the gate, and the gate then reads a model change, enqueues every Meeting with Kept Audio oldest first and overwrites the stamp with the model it walked for. A fresh install runs the same sequence on nothing and walks zero Meetings; the caller now treats `Some(0)` as silence rather than logging a re-run of nothing. `started_at` is written by SQLite's `strftime`, which is safe because nothing parses it (`state` does not read it). Tests: `schema` inverted its two not-registered gates into one pairing test and runs the real upgrade on a WeSpeaker-stamped History; `rerun` pins the stamp-to-walk step and the fresh-install `Some(0)` then `None`; `server` pins `Core::new` + the daemon's trigger enqueuing all of a populated History, and a fresh install showing nothing. The test fixtures that used to install the tables by hand now delete the stamp instead, so every test that wanted "no row" still starts from one. Removing the seed fails four tests and passes the rest, which is the failure mode this entry exists to name.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user's instruction, verbatim: *"register the migrations"* — given after Q226 ("register the migrations later") and Q227's recommendation to register soon. The seed and the `Some(0)` handling are the agent's, confirmed with the advisor as design A over a fabricated identity or a second migration; the identity seeded is the one `the_stored_identity_is_not_the_download_key` pinned until the swap and every fielded vector carries. This is the data-loss floor the brief held on the user's word, and the user released it.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q217, Q221 — where each says the tables are not in `MIGRATIONS` and the paths are unreachable in the field; everything else in both stands.
**Ref:** 2a30c79

## Q229 — diarization/12 — deviation

**Question:** Make the startup gate's swallowed `Err` visible to the Operator on a surface that already exists — the advisor named `rerunError` on the `diarize/status` re-run block, said to have been wired by `4d403d7` — now that a failure there means a wiped History whose walk did not start.
**Options considered:** — (escalated)
**Chosen:** —
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **The named surface does not exist.** There is no `rerunError` field anywhere in `crates/evertranscript-protocol` or the Electron client; `4d403d7` wired the counts block — `model`, `modelVersion`, `total`, `done`, `remaining`, `abandoned`, `cancelled`, `pausedForRecording` — and no error field with it. Nor can the block be given one without a protocol change, for a structural reason rather than a missing line: `Core::rerun_block` returns `None` unless `Rerun::requested()`, and in the exact failure case being discussed the row is still the wipe's stamp at `total` 0, so `requested()` is false and the whole `rerun` key is absent from the response. Attaching an error to a block that is not serialized would carry nothing. The alternatives are a new optional field on `DiarizeStatusResponse`, or a field on `DiarizeRerun` plus forcing the block to appear where it is currently absent — which is the "absent, not null, not a zeroed block" contract ADR-0028 and two tests pin. `StatusResponse.incomplete_copy_warning` is the only other free-text warning on the wire and means a different thing. Both routes are protocol changes, and the instruction was to stop and say so rather than add one, so the visibility half is the user's call. **What was applied instead, as unblocked work under the same instruction:** the retry property the advisor asked to have confirmed is real and is now asserted rather than argued. Nothing overwrites the stamp but `begin`'s own commit — `store::write` hands the job a bare connection and opens no transaction of its own, `begin` opens an `unchecked_transaction` that rolls back on drop, and `record` runs inside it — so a failure anywhere in the gate leaves the History as the wipe left it and the next start reads the same model change. `a_gate_that_fails_at_startup_is_retried_by_the_next_start` arranges the half-installed schema `installed`'s doc already names, boots through the failure, asserts nothing was enqueued and the stamp is still WeSpeaker, then repairs the schema and asserts the next start walks all three Meetings; recording the identity before the walk instead of after makes it fail. The `Err` arm's comment and message now say the same thing. So the cost of a failure is one session of degraded recognition, not a walk that never happens — which is the fact that decides how urgent the visibility is.
**Outcome:** escalated
**Ref:** cb8eacf

## Q230 — diarization/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q229 escalated how to make a failed startup gate visible, there being no field on the wire to carry it. Which shape, now that the protocol change is authorized?
**Options considered:** a new optional top-level `rerunError` on `DiarizeStatusResponse` / a field on `DiarizeRerun` plus forcing the block to appear / a server notification
**Chosen:** A new optional top-level field, `rerunError: Option<String>`, `skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none"` — so a Core that has never failed serializes exactly the bytes it did before, and ADR-0028's absent-block contract on `rerun` is untouched. Held in `Core` as a `Mutex<Option<String>>` for the session, written on every pass of `rerun_if_the_model_changed` — `Some(error.to_string())` on `Err`, `None` on any `Ok`, so a later start that works takes the message back rather than latching it. Not in the record, because the record already holds the fact that matters: the stamp survives a failure, so the walk is still owed and the next start asks again (Q229); what a restart loses is only the sentence, and a restart is what fixes the thing the sentence is about. The Electron client keeps it in its own state, `rerunStartupError`, deliberately apart from the existing `rerunError` there — which is this window failing to *read*, clears on the next poll, and would blank a Core-side failure on the next tick if the two were folded together. Rendered on the Registry below the re-run block, outside it, because `rerun` is absent in exactly this case. Copy in both locales: what was kept ("every word and every name") before what to do ("restarting will try again"), since the Operator reading it has just had every Voiceprint cleared.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The user delegated the three held items with, verbatim: *"do 1,2,3"*. The shape was the advisor's preference and is the one the evidence supports: `Core::rerun_block` returns `None` unless `Rerun::requested()`, which is false at the wipe's `total` 0, so the failure case is precisely the case with no block to hang a field on. Pinned by `a_gate_that_failed_is_reported_on_the_status_and_cleared_by_one_that_works` — half-install the schema, run the gate, repair, assert the message is on the status and names the missing table, assert `rerun` is `None` beside it, then run a gate that works and assert the field is gone and the walk is queued. The two byte-for-byte contract tests now assert `rerunError` is absent as well as `rerun`. Bindings regenerated through `ts-rs` and synced by the client's own `protocol:sync`; 230 strings, all locales complete.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q229 — which escalated this and is now answered; its finding about the surface stands as the reason for the shape.
**Ref:** 8369b62

## Q231 — diarization/07 — tradeoff

**Question:** Which matcher point does production use, now that the rate of exchange between a correct and a wrong attributed second has been delegated rather than given?
**Options considered:** 0.62/0.08 (shipped) / 0.80/0.00 / 0.30/0.00 — the points predeclared in Q182 and run in Q183
**Chosen:** 0.62/0.08 — unchanged, now by selection rather than inheritance. `MATCH_FLOOR` and `MATCH_MARGIN` keep their values and their doc comments say why. Read in the arm that ships and nowhere else: unconstrained, ReDimNet2 clustering with ReDimNet2 identity (the single-model control), on held-out AMI test, from the saved ledger `.scratch/diarization-independent/heldout-24.tsv`. No new measurement, no retuning on test, no point considered that was not predeclared. Denominators 25538.370s returning and 5175.554s new, fixed across all three rows. **0.30/0.00 is dominated** and needs no rate: against the shipped point it is 1755.870s worse on correct returning, 1780.500s worse on wrong, 149.550s worse on correct-new and 162.950s worse on false attachment. **0.80/0.00 is a trade**: correct returning 20324.570s against 20628.360s and wrong returning 2889.380s against 2927.080s, with correct-new and false attachment tied exactly at 4677.384s and 0.000s — so the shipped point buys 303.790s of correct returning for 37.700s of wrong, and only the rate decides it.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The user delegated with, verbatim: *"do 1,2,3"*, in reply to a list whose second item was this. **The rate assumed, as a number so it can be overruled as one: 4 seconds of correct returning per 1 second of wrong** — abstention scores zero, so a wrong second costs four times what an abstained second does, which respects M3's rule that a wrong attribution costs more than an unnamed one without pretending the ratio was measured. It was not; nothing on this corpus measures it, and it is a utility judgement in Q152's sense. At that rate the ranking is 0.62/0.08 (8920.040) then 0.80/0.00 (8767.050) then 0.30/0.00 (−609.630). **The choice does not turn on the figure**: the break-even is 8.06, so any rate below that gives the same order, and a reader who holds one wrong second to be worth more than eight correct ones — a far more conservative stance than M3's text supports — should overrule this to 0.80/0.00, which is the one substitution this entry invites. What the trade is in plain terms: moving from 0.80/0.00 to 0.62/0.08 converts 341.490s of abstention into 303.790s correct and 37.700s wrong, an 89.0% conversion. Limits carried from Q153 and Q183: three points, one arm, one corpus, one split; net changes only, so which abstaining seconds became correct and which became wrong is not known; newcomers are saturated on this split and discriminate nothing between the two candidates.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 3fc9625

## Q232 — diarization/06 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Is the ≥ 2.0-point DER adoption bar moot after Q226, or should it be recorded as the standing bar for any future embedding change?
**Options considered:** moot, close the field / adopt ≥ 2.0 points as standing / replace it with a reporting requirement that is not a single DER number
**Chosen:** Not adopted as a number, and the field is closed rather than filled. In its place, a standing requirement for any future embedding change: report **DER and all four recognition quantities** — correct returning, wrong returning, correct-new, newcomer false attachment — at points predeclared before any held-out number is read, in the clustering arm that actually ships, on a held-out split; and state the rate of exchange assumed, as a number, when the four do not point one way. A change that improves DER while losing correct returning at the shipped matcher point is not adopted on the DER alone.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Delegated by the user's *"do 1,2,3"*. Moot for the adoption that happened — Q226 was a human decision on the whole record, and a bar it did not consult cannot retroactively have gated it — but not moot going forward, because the question recurs at every embedding change. What the record forbids is making it a DER number: the same-window constraint moves WeSpeaker's DER by 5.13 points and ReDimNet2's by 0.68 and so **reverses which model wins on DER**, while recognition does not move with it and favours ReDimNet2 at every declared point (Q149, Q151, Q153, Q214 as corrected by Q216). A bar of "≥ 2.0 points of DER" is therefore satisfiable by a configuration that recognises people worse, which is the opposite of what the bar was for; and Q183 found no configuration dominating its controls on both, so no single scalar orders these outcomes. Recording the requirement rather than the number keeps what the bar was protecting — that a change be measured on a held-out split against predeclared points before it ships — without asserting a rate that has never been measured. This closes the last of ADR-0037's three reserved fields; Q231 answers the rate for the decision it was needed for, and does not generalise beyond it.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q139 — its open DER-bar field, which Q226 left standing; the model decision it escalated was already answered there.
**Ref:** 3fc9625

## Q235 — diarization/12 — deviation

**Question:** The instruction was to leave the host as found, and the shipped v1.1.1 app was running when the work began. Restart it?

The one listener still bound is the fleet's own reverse tunnel on 127.0.0.1:8317, which was there before this work and is opened by a launchd agent on another machine.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Restarting v1.1.1 would undo the migration the user asked for, which makes it the one reading of *leave the host as found* that contradicts the rest of the instruction. The embedding is resolved by a fixed filename with no version in it, and the registry entry says so deliberately: an installed copy still holding WeSpeaker's 26 535 549 bytes under that name *reads as `Corrupted` on size and is fetched afresh*. The mechanism runs in reverse here. v1.1.1 expects WeSpeaker's size, would find ReDimNet2's 18 045 013 bytes, would call the file corrupt and would re-fetch WeSpeaker over the top of it. v1.1.1 also predates the two migrations registered in Q228, so it would not wipe and re-run; it would take the lazy `stale_exemplars` path and rebuild all 24 ReDimNet2 Voiceprints back into WeSpeaker space from saved windows. The result is a History migrated twice and landing where it started, with a network fetch nobody asked for. The fix is to install a build from current `main` on that host, which is the user's call and not something to do unasked on their own machine.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 2978f20

## Q236 — diarization/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** The Operator came back from the re-run with no Voiceprint and lost their name on the four most recent Meetings. Is that the design, or a defect to fix?
**Options considered:** — (escalated)
**Chosen:** —
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The behaviour is what the code says it should do, and the cost is larger than the comments suggest, so whether it is acceptable is a product call rather than a reading of the source. What the source says: `reseed::plan` excludes the Operator from the eligible set, and `cluster.rs` excludes them again from the rebuilt set, both deliberately — the module doc reasons that the Operator *is rebuilt by the three channel rules alone (ADR-0029 as amended) — never from the previous model's guesses about which voice was theirs*. But the third of those three rules **is** the Voiceprint match, and nothing rebuilds the Voiceprint it needs, so a model change leaves only rules 1 and 2 standing. The measured cost on this History: the Operator held 366 segments across 10 Meetings before the walk and 483 segments across 6 after it. More speech, four fewer Meetings. In the walk's log the Operator was identified by rule 1, isolated mic, in exactly three Meetings, and the four Meetings that lost the name are the four most recent — so the Operator's own speech in the newest Meetings is now attributed to an unnamed pseudonym instead. The store shows the Operator holding 9 positive exemplars in the new space and a null Voiceprint. From the user's side this reads as their own name disappearing from the Meetings they are most likely to open. Two ways out, neither an agent's to pick: let the Operator relearn from their own attributed ranges like any other named Speaker, which is the thing the comment gives a reason against; or keep the exclusion and re-enrol the Operator explicitly after a model change, which needs a surface that does not exist. Recorded here rather than fixed because it changes who a biometric is learned from.
**Outcome:** escalated
**Ref:** 2978f20

## Q237 — diarization/12 — deviation

**Question:** The Operator was excluded from relearning, which after a model change left them with no Voiceprint and cost them their name on the four most recent Meetings. Remove the exclusion, or keep it and add a re-enrolment surface?
**Options considered:** let the Operator relearn from their own attributed ranges like any other named Speaker / keep the exclusion and build an explicit post-model-change re-enrolment
**Chosen:** Removed, at both sites. `reseed::plan` and `cluster::claims` each filtered the Operator out of the eligible set with the same four lines, and both filters are gone, so the Operator relearns from their own attributed ranges exactly as every other named Speaker does. **What does not change:** ADR-0029's three channel rules still decide who the Operator *is*. What changes is that rule 3's Voiceprint is rebuilt by the re-run rather than left for nothing to rebuild. **The boundary that stays**, and it is the one that made the exclusion look right: both paths read which Speaker *the record attributes* a segment to, never a cluster vector — so the Operator is relearned from their own words and never from a previous model's guess at which cluster was theirs. That distinction is the whole of `reseed`'s design and it applies to everybody; it never needed an Operator-shaped exception. Two tests pin it, `a_re_run_re_seeds_the_operators_voiceprint` in `reseed` and `the_operator_claims_a_cluster_like_any_other_named_speaker` in `cluster`, and each fails when its own exclusion is put back — checked by restoring both and watching exactly those two fail. The second replaces `the_operator_is_never_claimed_from_the_old_models_attributions`, which asserted the behaviour now withdrawn; its doc comment records what it used to say and why that changed rather than vanishing.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user chose between the two options Q236 escalated, verbatim: *'1 a 2 install'* — the `a` being the first of them. **ADR-0029 needs no amendment**, which was worth checking before assuming one: its text never claims the Operator's Voiceprint is not rebuilt from attributions. The amendment of 2026-08-27 says the Operator is *matched on the mic channel by Voiceprint — bootstrapped from the dominant mic voice over the first Meetings*, and the document contains no mention of a rebuild, a re-run, a relearn or a model change at all. The "never from the previous model's guesses" rule was asserted only in the two module docs removed here, which cited the ADR for something it does not say. Both docs now carry what replaced it and why. `cargo fmt` clean; clippy reports one warning, `chunks_exact_to_as_chunks` at `server.rs:4947`, which is present at HEAD with these changes stashed and in a file this does not touch. Core lib suite 665 passed, 1 failed: `detect::macos::tests::a_real_microphone_hold_is_visible_to_the_detector`, which also fails at HEAD with these changes stashed and in isolation, because the microphone TCC grant is keyed on a test binary path that a rebuild changes. It is an environment failure, not a regression, and is reported rather than filtered.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q236 — its escalation, which asked exactly this and left it to the user.
**Ref:** 23602b3

## Q238 — diarization/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** How is Q237's fix verified against the migrated History on `macbook-pro-nickel`, given that the instruction named "Registry re-run request or `begin()` via the product's own surface, not a hand-edited stamp"?
**Options considered:** `diarize/run` per Meeting / induce a real model change by swapping the embedding file / seed the gate's stamp on a throwaway copy of the History / hand-edit the stamp on the real History
**Chosen:** — (escalated: the named path does not exist, and the substitute needs a human's word)
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The user released this with, verbatim: *'1 a 2 install'*. **Neither form of the named path exists once a backlog is drained**, which was worth establishing before reaching for a substitute. First, there is no Registry re-run request: the whole protocol surface for this is `diarize/status`, `diarize/run`, `diarize/cancel` and `diarize/rerunCancel` — a cancel, and no start. Second, `begin_if_the_model_changed` has exactly one production caller, the startup gate in `Core`; every other call site is a test. Third, `diarize/run` reaches the ordinary path rather than the re-run's: `reseed::plan` is read only when `rerun::is_bulk_work` is true, and that is a row in `diarize_rerun_backlog`, which the walk of Q234 drained. So a per-Meeting request re-diarizes without relearning and cannot exercise the half of Q237 that rebuilds a Voiceprint. **Nor can a model change be induced by swapping the file**, which is the tidy trick this otherwise invites: the registry pins `diarize-embedding.onnx` to 18 045 013 bytes, so putting WeSpeaker's 26 535 549 there makes the installed build read it as `Corrupted` on size and fetch ReDimNet2 back — the same mechanism Q235 relies on, running in the other direction. That leaves a seeded stamp, and the substitute prepared is the narrowest form of it: **a throwaway copy of the History** at a scratch path, reached by `EVERTRANSCRIPT_HISTORY_DIR` with its own `EVERTRANSCRIPT_RUNTIME_DIR`, whose `diarize_rerun` row is set to WeSpeaker's identity at total 0 — the shape `MODEL_CHANGE_RERUN` itself writes. The real gate, the real `begin()`, the real `reseed` and the real audio then run; only the trigger is seeded, and the Operator's own record is read and never written. **Escalated rather than applied because the instruction ruled out a hand-edited stamp and this is one**, differing only in being confined to a copy — and because repairing the real History afterwards would need the same seed on the record itself, which is a write to the Operator's own data that no test result can authorise. What is not in doubt: the fix is pinned by two tests, each mutation-checked, and takes effect on the next genuine model change whether or not this verification runs.
**Outcome:** escalated
**Ref:** 1860132

## Q239 — diarization/12 — deviation

**Question:** The install was to happen through a watcher that waited for `macbook-pro-nickel` to wake. The user cancelled that. What happens to the script copies already suspended on that host?
**Options considered:** kill the watcher and leave the host alone / kill it and arm a cleanup that only kills, never installs / try to reach the host and disarm synchronously
**Chosen:** All three, in that order of preference, and the second is what is running. The installing watcher is stopped. One direct attempt to disarm the host synchronously found it still unreachable. In its place is a kill-only cleanup whose entire remote effect is `pkill` of my own two scripts, deleting those two files, releasing their lock, and killing the two `caffeinate` invocations I started, matched on their exact durations so a `caffeinate` of the Operator's own is not touched. It installs nothing, launches nothing, and reports the host's state once. Nothing else on that machine is written to.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user's instruction, verbatim: *'kill the watcher, don't install unattended'*. Killing the watcher alone would not have honoured the second half. **Three copies of the install script are suspended on that host**, launched at 10:44:14, 10:44:30 and 10:44:46 before the single-instance lock existed — my own error: the launching loop treated a `cat` of a status file that did not exist yet as a failed launch and retried twice. Suspended, not killed, because the machine slept rather than shut down, so they resume where they were when it next wakes. Each would have gone on to replace `/Applications/EverTranscript.app`, which is exactly the unattended install being ruled out, and two completing at once would race on the same `rm -rf` and `cp`. The stopped watcher was also the only thing that would have killed them, so removing it without a replacement would have raised the risk rather than lowered it. What is not at risk either way: the Operator's History. The script reads it, copies it, and writes only to a scratch directory, and the backup from Q234 is untouched at `~/EverTranscript-backups/pre-migration-20260917-045650/`. **Left undone and reported rather than attempted:** the install itself, and Q238's verification. Both need a host that stays awake.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** a2ae1d4

## Q240 — diarization/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** How does anyone ask for a bulk re-run once the backlog has been drained, when the startup gate is the only caller and cannot notice a model already recorded?
**Options considered:** seed the gate's stamp by hand (Q238's prepared substitute) / `diarize/run` per Meeting / add the missing protocol method over `rerun::begin` / wait for the next genuine model change
**Chosen:** Added `diarize/rerunRequest` over `store::rerun::begin` — the start the protocol was missing beside the `diarize/rerunCancel` it already carried.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user released this with, verbatim: *'yes, do 1'*. Q238 escalated this as "the named path does not exist". That was half right, and the wrong half is the one that mattered: the mechanism exists and is documented as the active entry point. `rerun::begin` reads *"Asks for a re-run outright… The entry point for a transition that knows History has to be walked — a wipe, or a deliberate re-derivation — and it does not consult the stored identity"*, naming `begin_if_the_model_changed` there as *"the passive form [which] refuses exactly that case"*. What was absent was a **surface**: a fully implemented, documented and tested function whose only caller was the startup gate, while the protocol carried a re-run cancel and no re-run start. Building it is therefore the instruction's own *"`begin()` via the product's own surface"* rather than a substitute for it, and it retires the hand-edited stamp Q238 was escalated over. The method is symmetric with the cancel beside it, answers the same `DiarizeStatusResponse`, and records this build's identity — the same pair the gate uses — so a re-run asked for here leaves History stamped exactly as an upgrade to the same model would have left it. Two departures from the gate are deliberate: a failure is returned rather than logged, because somebody asked and is waiting for the answer; and a stale `rerunError` from a failed gate attempt is cleared on success, since the walk it reported as owed is the one now running. Pinned by two tests, both mutation-checked by pointing the method at the passive gate instead — exactly those two fail and no others. 667 lib tests pass; the single failure is the pre-existing mic-detector test, whose TCC grant is keyed on a test binary path that any rebuild changes, and which fails at `HEAD` too. Clippy is unchanged at its one pre-existing warning.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q238 — its escalation asked how to re-run a drained backlog and concluded the named path did not exist. The path existed; only the protocol method was missing, and it is now there.
**Ref:** 3e56a1c

## Q241 — interactive/nickel-hygiene — deviation

**Question:** `macbook-pro-nickel` carries a loaded LaunchAgent, `com.evertranscript.core`, able to start the installed v1.1.1 Core at login. Disable it, against the standing instruction to leave that host as found?
**Options considered:** leave it and rely on nobody logging in / disable it persistently / delete the plist / boot it out for this session only
**Chosen:** Disabled persistently with `launchctl disable gui/<uid>/com.evertranscript.core`, keyed on the label so it survives a reboot and a rewritten plist. The plist is copied to `~/EverTranscript-backups/` first and otherwise left in place.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user's instruction, verbatim: *'disable that launch agent'*. Found by the `/tmp` script audit, which was looking for something else: `launchctl list` reports `-	1	com.evertranscript.core` — not running, last exit status 1. It matters because the installed app is still v1.1.1 while this morning's migration left ReDimNet2 at `diarize-embedding.onnx`, 18 045 013 bytes, under the fixed filename that build expects WeSpeaker's 26 535 549 at. v1.1.1 therefore reads it as `Corrupted` on size and re-fetches WeSpeaker over it, rebuilding every Voiceprint back into the old space. A login item makes that happen with nobody opening the app — the one route to losing the migration that care inside this session could not have caught. `disable` rather than deleting the plist, because it is reversible with `launchctl enable`, leaves the product's own file alone, and is keyed on the label, so it holds even if the app rewrites its login item. A running Core is booted out **only** when `status` reports `Idle`: a `bootout` mid-capture would take the Meeting with it, and no verification is worth that. Confirmation is pending the host waking, which it does for seconds at a time; the poller retries every two seconds.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** d73ce19

## Q242 — interactive/journal-refs — deviation

**Question:** 201 entries read `**Ref:** (pending)` and five more cited commits that no longer exist on any branch. What should each cite, and is editing that many lines of an append-only journal legitimate?
**Options considered:** leave them / backfill the introducing commit for each / backfill only recent entries / repoint the five orphans at their surviving equivalents
**Chosen:** Backfilled all 201 with the commit whose diff added that entry, and repointed the five orphans at the rebased commits carrying the same work: `ba8a491`→`cfd1077`, `058bcad`→`2880ba6`, `c72bb74`→`ff74f59`, `8dd6781`→`f2d9b48`, `c175ad0`→`cbc82a5`. Nothing but `**Ref:**` lines changed.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user asked for both, verbatim *'backfill the rest of the refs'* and *'fix those five too'*. Legitimate because a `Ref` is an address rather than content — the same reasoning this skill already applies to renumbering repair — and because a dangling address is worse than a blank one: it looks resolvable and is not. **Two anchors, and the weaker one was wrong seven times.** Matching each entry by its `**Question:**` line resolved 194 of 201 but mis-assigned 7, because a revision appends a new entry asking the same question and because entries were renumbered when the two journals were merged; the anchor that settles it is the commit whose diff added the entry's header. `Q133` and `Q134` appear in no ordinary diff at all: they were **created by** the merge `d0adc48` as it reconciled the forked journals, and `git log -p` omits merge commits, so the first pass could not see them. **The five orphans were a different problem.** Their objects exist but are reachable from nothing — a later rebase left them behind — and their entries had been logged in separate record-only commits (`2146b3b`, `e5f0c13`, `edb7458`, `e6ffefe`, `66e4fb8`, touching only `DECISIONS.md` and NOTES), so those `Ref`s were pointing at the *work*, not the entry. Repointing preserves that intent rather than changing what the field means: each survivor was confirmed by identical `git patch-id --stable` and an identical file list, not by subject alone. Their prose citations in `NOTES.md` and two ticket files were updated in step, since a hash nobody can resolve is not a citation. **Verified:** 0 non-`Ref` lines changed, 2802 lines before and after, 241 entries and 0 numbering breaks, 0 pending, and all 226 commit refs across 128 distinct hashes resolve and are ancestors of `HEAD` — as does every 7-character hash cited in NOTES and the issue files.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** c8ead35

## Q243 — issues/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q238 escalated because no re-run could be triggered on a drained backlog without hand-seeding a stamp. `diarize/rerunRequest` (Q240) removed that. Does the Operator earn a Voiceprint back on the real History now that Q236 lets them relearn, and does the walk drive end to end from the protocol?
**Options considered:** seed an old stamp on a copy (Q238's substitute) / drive `diarize/rerunRequest` against a copy / run against the real History
**Chosen:** `diarize/rerunRequest` against a throwaway copy on `macbook-pro-nickel`. **The walk is clean and the Operator earns a Voiceprint back — and the four Meetings they had already lost do not come back.**
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Released by the user's *'do 1'*, choosing verification without the Q235 install. No stamp was seeded: `rerunRequest` is the active entry point, so nothing had to be faked, which is what made Q238's escalation unnecessary rather than merely satisfied. Built `a620adb` on the host and ran it against `EVERTRANSCRIPT_HISTORY_DIR` on a copy, with `EVERTRANSCRIPT_RUNTIME_DIR` and — new against the earlier design — `EVERTRANSCRIPT_APP_SUPPORT_DIR` at a scratch models directory, so the Q227 re-fetch could not reach the real artifacts even if it fired. **The walk:** queued all 10 Meetings, walked 10 of 10 oldest-first in ~16.5 minutes, one relearn logged per Meeting with 82–406 exemplars, `abandoned` 0, `cancelled` false, backlog and queue empty, `state: idle`, and no `rerunError` key at any point. **The Operator:** `redimnet2-b3`/`1` where Q234's walk left null, so 7 of 8 named Speakers hold a Voiceprint; the eighth has 0 attributed segments and nothing to learn from, which is correct rather than a miss. **What did not happen:** the Operator stood at 6 Meetings before and 6 after — Q237 measured 10 before the first walk — while segments rose 483 to 493, and every other named Speaker's count is identical to the digit, so the 10 came from previously unattributed speech and nothing was taken from anyone. So Q236's fix restores the Operator's *eligibility* to hold a biometric; it does not recover speech a pseudonym already owns. Read-only census of the real History shows why this is the expected shape: the four Meetings missing the Operator are the four most recent (2026-09-14 to 2026-09-16), and in each one a single pseudonym holding its own Voiceprint owns 24 to 96 segments. A Voiceprint cut from exactly the segments in question will match them better than one learned from six older Meetings, so the pseudonym keeps them. That the pseudonym is the Operator's voice is consistent with the pattern rather than proven — the copy was deleted at cleanup and the claim was not tested directly. **Consequence:** the user-visible harm Q237 named, the Operator's name absent from the Meetings they are most likely to open, is not closed by relearning alone and needs the other half Q237 offered — an explicit re-enrol, or a merge of pseudonym into Speaker — which is a surface that does not exist and a product call. **Left as found:** real History 10 Meetings and 24 Voiceprints before and after, the real embedding's sha unchanged, the installed v1.1.1 never started, `/Applications` never written, no processes left, scratch deleted, and a fresh backup at `~/EverTranscript-backups/pre-verify-20260917-134121.db`. Ticket 12's last criterion was already met by Q234 and its checkbox is merely unticked; this entry answers the narrower question Q236 opened after it.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q238 — the substitute it escalated over is moot; the active trigger removed the need to seed anything
**Ref:** 8b2d4aa

## Q244 — issues/12 — deviation

**Question:** Q243 cites Q236 as the fix that let the Operator relearn and Q237 as the entry that raised the cost. They are the other way round, and it also claims to supersede Q238, which Q240 already superseded. What is the record?
**Options considered:** leave it / edit Q243 in place / append a correction that supersedes it
**Chosen:** Corrected here, and Q243 left as written. **Q236 is the escalation** — *"The Operator came back from the re-run with no Voiceprint and lost their name on the four most recent Meetings. Is that the design, or a defect to fix?"*, `Outcome: escalated`, Ref `2978f20`. **Q237 is the fix** — the exclusion removed from `reseed::plan` and `cluster::claims`, `Outcome: applied`, Ref `23602b3`. Every sentence in Q243 naming one means the other. Its `Supersedes: Q238` is withdrawn: Q240 superseded Q238 already, and one escalation cannot be closed twice. **The measurement Q243 reports is unaffected** — no number, host, or verdict changes; only the entries they are attributed to.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Appended rather than edited because the journal is append-only and this is content, not a `Ref` address or a numbering repair — the two sanctioned exceptions, and neither covers a wrong citation. The file's own precedent is this shape: Q229 corrected Q226's claim by superseding it rather than rewriting it. Caught while ticking ticket 12's last checkbox, where NOTES.md had the pair the right way round and the entry did not. **Two related repairs made in the same change, both to editable files rather than to the journal:** the ticket's new prose carried the same swap and is fixed, and NOTES.md's line on Q237 claimed the re-run was *"re-verified on the same host against an installed build"* — which never happened. Q237's own text makes no such claim, no install has occurred, and Q235 is still open; that sentence now records what Q243 actually did, on a copy through `diarize/rerunRequest`, together with the finding that the four lost Meetings do not return. A claim of verification against a build that was never installed is the kind of error that reads as evidence later, which is why it is corrected now rather than noted.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q243 — its citations of Q236 and Q237 are transposed throughout, and its `Supersedes` line duplicates Q240's
**Ref:** b9b0d45

## Q245 — issues/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q243 inferred that the pseudonym owning the Operator's four missing Meetings is the Operator's own voice, on pattern alone, and the product call between a pseudonym merge and an explicit re-enrol hinges on it. Is it?
**Options considered:** leave it inferred / compare the Operator's relearned Voiceprint against those Speakers / compare per-exemplar
**Chosen:** Tested, and **the question cannot be answered as posed, because the Operator's own record holds two different voices.** Their 9 exemplars split into two mutually orthogonal groups — `{0,2,3,6}` at pairwise 0.83–0.96 and `{1,4,5,7,8}` at 0.55–0.89, with everything across the split between −0.12 and +0.05. All 9 are positive, machine-sourced, `redimnet2-b3`/`1`, and every one is from the **`mic` channel**. Both groups occur *inside the same Meeting*: exemplars 1 and 2 are both mic windows of Meeting `01a083ad` and score −0.10 against each other. So "the Operator's voice" is not one thing in this store.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Asked for by the user, who named the doubt in Q243 and the access to settle it — on a copy, nothing installed, real History, `/Applications` and TCC untouched; the measurement below needed none of those, being read-only against the real database with `mode=ro`. **The first pass was an artifact and is recorded because it is the trap here.** Averaging the 9 exemplars gives an anchor that is a blend of two people, against which the Operator's *own* exemplars score only 0.5793–0.6905 — so two pseudonyms landing at 0.6256 and 0.6374 read as "at or above the 0.62 floor" while being indistinguishable from the Operator's own evidence. A centroid over dissimilar exemplars cannot discriminate, and a number taken against it means nothing. **Per-exemplar scoring separates cleanly**, which is what makes the result usable: pseudonym `…d859b11a42e1` (81 segments) matches group `{0,2,3,6}` at 0.92–0.96 and group `{1,4,5,7,8}` at −0.08 to 0.01; pseudonyms `…d893116fdf4e` (74), `…cd5b6208b876` (61) and `…94a7eccc7160` (10) match the other group at 0.56–0.90 and the first at −0.11 to −0.02. The named controls confirm the metric rather than the hypothesis: Jack Ahn scores at most 0.2572 across all nine and Hong Li at most 0.3890, so a different person is unambiguous at this floor. **What this means:** the Speakers owning the four Meetings are the same two voices already inside the Operator's exemplar set — so Q243's inference is half right and wrong where it matters, since one of those voices is somebody else. **It settles the product call, against the option that looked safer.** A merge of any of these pseudonyms into the Operator would cement a second person's speech into the Operator's identity under a confirmed name, which is worse than the missing name it repairs. A re-enrol has to choose which of the two groups is the Operator, and nothing in the vectors says which — that needs a person to listen to two ten-second windows. **The likely root cause, supported but not proven:** every exemplar comes from the isolated-mic channel, and a laptop mic in a room carries whoever is in the room, so crediting that channel wholesale to the Operator enrols the room. That is a hypothesis about the rule, not a reading of it; what is measured is the contamination, not its source. **Two errors of my own in the first pass, both fixed before any conclusion:** Speaker ids were truncated to 12 characters, and the Operator `01a08356-b3d7-76d0-aba7-2d83434fb6ac` shares 28 characters with Jack Ahn `01a08356-b3d7-76d0-aba7-2d621bbb86a9`, so one row was labelled with the other's name; and the exemplars were averaged before `is_negative` was checked, which happened to be harmless here — all 9 are positive — but was not established at the time.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q243 — its closing inference is tested here and does not hold as stated; the walk, the Voiceprint result and the four-Meetings finding it reports are unaffected
**Ref:** 3a9fd51

## Q246 — issues/12 — deviation

**Question:** Q245 named the cause as the isolated-mic rule crediting the whole mic channel to the Operator, "supported but not proven". Prove or refute it; and does the Voiceprint mint path check that its exemplars agree before stamping a name on them?
**Options considered:** leave it supported / prove it / refute it
**Chosen:** **Refuted — rule 1 never fired on this History — and replaced with a proven correlation. The mint path checks nothing, which is a real defect and is not confined to this host.**
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Asked for by the user, read-only throughout; real History, `/Applications` and TCC untouched. **The refutation is flat.** Rule 1 is `operator.rs:158`, `if facts.mic_isolated == Some(true)`, and it does take every distinct mic cluster with no threshold — its own doc says *"every voice on it is the Operator, confirmed without any act"*. But `meetings.mic_isolated` is **NULL in 9 of the 10 Meetings and `0` in the tenth**, so the condition was never met and that rule cannot be what happened. Q245's stated cause is wrong. **What did happen is rule 2**, `Identified::Dominant`, which is thresholded (`DOMINANCE` 0.80, `DOMINANCE_MARGIN` 0.5, `MIN_OPERATOR_MS` 20 000) and which the mic-channel totals for `01a083ad` clear comfortably — the Operator's cluster holds 468s of 560s voiced, an 0.836 share against a 0.089 runner-up. Rule 2 names **one whole cluster**, and that cluster is what carries the contamination. **The correlation that replaces the guess is exact.** Of the Operator's 9 exemplars, the 4 in the coherent group `{0,2,3,6}` fall in windows where the system channel is **silent** in 3 cases; all **5 of 5** in the other group `{1,4,5,7,8}` fall in windows overlapping system-channel speech. The test Meeting shows it directly: exemplar 2 at 832–842s has silence on the far end and matches one pseudonym at 0.956, while exemplar 1 at 2631–2641s runs underneath Ming Chen speaking on the system channel for 30s. So the second voice is **double-talk** — mic audio captured while the far end was speaking. **It is not simply the far end leaking**, which is worth stating because that was the obvious next guess: group B scores 0.02–0.13 against Ming Chen's own Voiceprint and at most 0.30 against any named Speaker, so it is neither the Operator's clean speech nor a clean copy of whoever was talking. Whether that is AEC residue of the far end or the Operator's own voice distorted by cancellation during double-talk cannot be told from vectors, and is not needed for what follows. **The defect, which is the part that generalises.** `cluster::centroid` (`cluster.rs:573`) filters negatives and empty vectors, weights by `voiced_ms`, averages and L2-normalizes — and tests nothing about whether the vectors agree. Its single mint site is `cluster.rs:687`, `match (centroid(&history), evidence.last())` → `speakers::set_voiceprint`, which `adopt_rebuilt` — the Q237 relearn path — routes through. A grep for coherence, spread, outlier or dispersion across `cluster.rs` and `reseed.rs` finds nothing. So **relearn will mint a Voiceprint by averaging mutually orthogonal exemplars**, and did: the resulting vector represents neither voice, which is why the Operator's own exemplars score only 0.5793–0.6905 against it. This is not specific to `macbook-pro-nickel` or to the Operator — the same mint serves every Speaker on every host, and any Speaker whose attributed segments carry two voices gets the same blend. The Operator is merely the most exposed, because rule 2 hands them a whole cluster. **The minimal guard, named and not built:** at the mint site, before `set_voiceprint`, measure the usable exemplars against the centroid they produce and refuse rather than stamp when they disagree — falling through to the `clear_voiceprint` branch that already exists there for "no vector this Speaker's record supports". That adds no new state, no new table and no new call site. A larger variant would keep the dominant coherent subset and mint from that, which loses less but is no longer minimal. **What is deliberately left open: the threshold, and the choice between refusing and subsetting.** Both decide whether a Speaker silently loses recognition or silently keeps a blurred identity, which is the same kind of judgement as `MATCH_FLOOR` and belongs to the user, not to me.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q245 — its closing hypothesis about the isolated-mic rule is refuted here; its measurement of the two orthogonal groups stands unchanged
**Ref:** 13c8425

## Q247 — issues/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q246's guard scores exemplars against the centroid they produce, but a centroid over dissimilar exemplars is the blur Q245 found cannot discriminate. Which measure and aggregation actually separates the contaminated Operator from every named Speaker the guard must not refuse?
**Options considered:** min exemplar-vs-centroid / mean exemplar-vs-centroid / fraction below a floor / min pairwise / mean pairwise
**Chosen:** Measured, not chosen — the threshold and refuse-vs-subset remain the user's. **`min` exemplar-vs-centroid inverts and must not be used; the two centroid-free pairwise measures separate cleanly, and mean pairwise is the one to hang a threshold on.**
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Asked for by the user. Read-only, `mode=ro`, replicating `cluster::refresh_voiceprint` exactly rather than approximating it — exemplars `ORDER BY id`, restricted to the newest exemplar's model space, negatives and empties dropped, newest `MAX_EXEMPLARS` 32, weighted by `voiced_ms.max(1)`, averaged, L2-normalized. **A correction this produced:** Q243 and Q245 reported the Operator's exemplars at 0.5793–0.6905 against their centroid, which was computed *unweighted*; against the product's own weighted centroid the figures are min **0.5142**, mean **0.6056**. The straddle the user identified is real and slightly worse than recorded. **The decisive result is that `min` exemplar-vs-centroid inverts:** the Operator scores 0.5142 while the weakest control — a second Speaker row also named Ming Chen — scores **0.4170**, so any floor that refuses the Operator on this measure refuses a legitimate named Speaker first. That is the measure the guard as named in Q246 would most naturally use, and it is the one that does not work. **What separates, Operator against the worst-case control:** mean exemplar-vs-centroid 0.6056 vs 0.7782, margin 0.1726; fraction below 0.62 0.5556 vs 0.1250, margin 0.4306; min pairwise −0.1216 vs 0.1818, margin 0.3034; mean pairwise 0.3190 vs 0.6143, margin 0.2953. **Mean pairwise is the recommendation** on three grounds: it never forms a centroid, so Q245's objection cannot apply to it; it is not a single-worst-pair statistic, so one noisy observation cannot condemn a Speaker; and it needs one threshold rather than two, where "fraction below a floor" needs a floor *and* a fraction. Its admissible band is wide — any value in (0.3190, 0.6143) separates every row measured here. **Three caveats that bear on where in that band to sit.** The Operator has 9 exemplars against the controls' 32, so their statistics rest on 36 pairs against 496 — which cuts in the separation's favour, since the controls had thirteen times the opportunity to produce a low pair and still stayed above 0.18. The weakest control is the duplicate "Ming Chen" row, whose 0.6143 is well below the other four named Speakers (0.6641 to 0.8085) and which may be mildly contaminated itself; excluding it widens the margin to 0.3451, and whether it should be excluded is a question about that Speaker, not about the guard. And every number here comes from **one** History of ten Meetings, so the band is evidence that a threshold exists, not a measurement of where it generalises.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 544c548

## Q248 — issues/12 — deviation

**Question:** The confirmatory walk minted the Operator's Voiceprint through the product's own relearn. What does that Voiceprint actually recognize?
**Options considered:** —
**Chosen:** **It recognizes the wrong person.** The relearned Voiceprint scores **0.6761** and **0.6724** against the two `Ming Chen` rows — above the 0.62 match floor — and **0.3473** against pseudonym `…d859b11a42e1`, which the per-exemplar work of Q245 and Q246 identified as the Operator's own clean voice. So the vector filed under the Operator's confirmed name matches a different participant and fails to match the Operator. Three of 27 Voiceprints in the store reach the floor against it: the Operator's own row at 1.0000, and both Ming Chen rows.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The walk drove `diarize/rerunRequest` on a second throwaway copy, 10 of 10 Meetings, and the anchor here is the product's own `set_voiceprint` output rather than anything reconstructed. **The contamination grew rather than shrank, because Q237's fix enlarged what feeds it.** Before the fix the Operator held 9 exemplars; after it, one per attributed segment — **483**, all positive, all machine-sourced. Their coherence is worse than the 9: mean pairwise **0.3674** against 0.3190, and the weakest exemplar sits at **0.0155** against its own centroid where the 9 bottomed out at 0.5142. `MAX_EXEMPLARS` is 32 and `centroid` takes the **newest** 32, so a Voiceprint built from 483 observations reflects only the most recent Meeting that contributed any — which is how a single badly-attributed Meeting captures the whole identity. The product's own Voiceprint agrees with the unweighted centroid of all 483 at only 0.6429, which is the cap and the weighting pulling in different directions. **Why this matters beyond the measurement:** the walk log shows `operator_rule=Discriminant(2)`, `Identified::Recognized`, firing on Meeting `01a08df9`, so rule 3 is already attributing speech on this vector. A Voiceprint that matches Ming Chen above the floor and the Operator below it does not merely fail to recognize — it stands ready to name the Operator's record over another participant's voice, and ADR-0008 as amended makes a confirmed Voiceprint outrank an unconfirmed one when matching, so it wins the ties it should lose. **This sharpens Q247's open choice rather than deciding it.** Refuse and subset are no longer symmetric options: a blended Voiceprint is not an identity that is merely vague, it is one that is confidently wrong about somebody else, which argues that whatever threshold is chosen should fail closed. The threshold and the choice remain the user's. **Qualification, stated because the evidence is gone:** the copy is deleted, so which Meetings supplied the newest 32 exemplars was not determined, and the claim that the cap is what let Ming Chen in is inference from the cap's existence rather than a reading of those 32 rows. Reproducing it needs another walk. **Left as found:** the real History is untouched at 10 Meetings, 24 Voiceprints and 1458 exemplars, the Operator's own Voiceprint there is still absent, the installed app is still v1.1.1, no scratch directories and no processes remain.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 56b7c92

## Q249 — issues/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q248 inferred from the cap's existence that `MAX_EXEMPLARS` is what let Ming Chen into the Operator's Voiceprint, and said so was inference rather than a reading of the rows. Do the rows bear it out?
**Options considered:** —
**Chosen:** **Borne out, exactly.** The weighted centroid of the newest 32 reproduces the stored Voiceprint at **1.000000**; the weighted centroid of all 483 matches it at only **0.516952**. All 32 come from **one Meeting**, `01a08e16` of 2026-09-10, and from a contiguous tail of it — windows between 4325s and 5009s. **All 32 of 32 overlap system-channel speech**, with a mean cosine to Ming Chen of **0.6444**, against 0.3473 for the Operator's own clean voice.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** A third walk on a throwaway copy, same protocol — nothing installed, `/Applications` and TCC untouched, the real History read `mode=ro` — with cleanup made explicit so the rows could be read before the copy went. **The apparent contradiction from the smoke test is resolved, and it was mine.** Running the same analysis over the real History's 9 exemplars had shown double-talk windows scoring only 0.1029 against Ming Chen, which I flagged as cutting against Q248. It does not: those 9 span six Meetings with different voices on the far end, while these 32 are one stretch of one Meeting in which the far-end speaker was Ming Chen. Different Meetings, not a contradiction. **The sharper statement the rows support** is that the cap does not sample a Speaker's history at all. Exemplars are read `ORDER BY id`, the ids are UUIDv7 minted at insert, and `centroid` takes `.rev().take(32)` — so the newest 32 are the last rows written, which is the tail of the last Meeting that contributed any. One Meeting's ending decided this identity, and every window in it had the far end talking. **This narrows Q247's band, which is the part that bears on the user's choice.** Those 32 are mutually more coherent than the contaminated 9, because they are all the same far-end voice: mean pairwise **0.4426** against the 9's 0.3190. Q247's admissible band was (0.3190, 0.6143); a threshold below 0.4426 passes these 32 and mints the wrong Voiceprint anyway, so requiring the guard to catch the failure that actually happened narrows the band to **(0.4426, 0.6143)** — width 0.1717 rather than 0.2953. **And it raises a design question the guard's threshold cannot answer:** the full 483-row record scores 0.3674 mean pairwise, well clear of the weakest control, so a guard that scores the Speaker's whole record rather than the capped slice the mint consumes sees the incoherence plainly and keeps the wider band. Where the guard reads is therefore as consequential as what it reads for, and both remain the user's to settle along with the threshold and refuse-versus-subset. Nothing was built.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 09cd84d

## Q250 — issues/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q246–Q249 exist only in this journal. NOTES.md still reads ticket 12 as all criteria met and names only Q135/Q145 as escalated, so a reader of NOTES would not learn that the mint has no agreement check, that it matched the wrong participant on the real History, or that a guard waits on three user choices. Where should the defect be recorded?
**Options considered:** leave it journalled only / a note on the ticket 12 row alone / an escalation entry in NOTES plus a ticket file
**Chosen:** All three surfaces, as the user directed: an entry under *Escalated, still open* in NOTES.md, a one-line pointer on the ticket 12 row, and a new held-for-user ticket `issues/13-a-voiceprint-is-not-minted-from-exemplars-that-disagree.md`. **No code, no threshold.**
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** Asked for by the user in those terms; the judgement recorded here is only how the three surfaces divide the material, since the decision to record it was theirs. **The journal is not where a reader looks.** `DECISIONS.md` answers *why was this decided* for someone who already knows the decision exists; NOTES.md is the entry point, and its two indexes both read clean — the ticket table says 12 is done and the escalation section names only the archived branch's `Q135`/`Q145`. A defect discovered *while verifying* a ticket's criteria is invisible to both, because it is not a criterion. **The split.** The ticket 12 row gets one sentence and no numbers: its job is to stop the row reading as closed, and a row already 1 200 words long should not grow an analysis. The NOTES entry carries what a reader needs without opening anything else — the mechanism (`centroid` tests nothing; `.rev().take(32)` over UUIDv7 ids takes a contiguous tail, not a sample), the evidence that it is real (0.6761/0.6724 against the two `Ming Chen` rows against 0.3473 against the Operator's own voice), the three choices as a table, both bands, and the warning that `min` exemplar-vs-centroid inverts. The ticket file carries acceptance criteria, the caveats, and the case for each option, because that is what someone picking the work up needs and not what someone orienting needs. **One thing is stated in all three places deliberately:** that this is not a `macbook-pro-nickel` finding.
