The same mint serves every Speaker on every host, and a reader who meets the defect on the laptop's row could reasonably conclude otherwise. **Ticket 13's criteria are unticked and its first criterion is the three choices themselves**, so the ticket cannot read as ready-for-agent — it is `held for user`, matching how 05 and 12 record their own held activation rather than inventing a status. **What was not done:** no guard, no threshold, no default picked for refuse-versus-subset or for where the guard reads. Q247's recommendation of mean pairwise is carried across as a recommendation, not as a selection.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** ad0c372

## Q251 — issues/14 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q249 found the cap takes a contiguous tail rather than a sample. Spec that as its own ticket and measure, read-only and without building, what spreading the newest 32 across a Speaker's Meetings would have produced on the real History.
**Options considered:** fold it into ticket 13 / a separate ticket
**Chosen:** **A separate ticket, 14, because it needs no threshold and can land without 13 — but the measurement says it is not free, and the two interact.** Five Speakers exceed the cap; three of them draw their whole identity from one or two of the four-to-seven Meetings they were heard in. Spreading changes **no** identity on this History, **lowers** coherence in every case, and **narrows ticket 13's band** from (0.4426, 0.6143) to (0.4426, 0.5846).
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Asked for by the user in those terms — read-only, no build. Run `mode=ro` against the real History on `macbook-pro-nickel`, replicating `cluster::centroid` exactly and comparing three selections of the same size: the tail-32 the product mints, a stratified 32 (round-robin across contributing Meetings, newest-first within each — offered as one candidate rule, not a chosen one), and all usable rows. **Separate ticket** because the two defects are independent: 13 is that nothing checks agreement, 14 is how the 32 are chosen, and either fixes without the other. 14 is a selection rule rather than a policy, so unlike 13 nothing in it is the user's to threshold — which is why it is marked buildable and 13 is marked held. **The narrowing is the normal case.** Jack Ahn holds 888 exemplars across 7 Meetings and his tail-32 spans **1**; Hong Li 152 across 4, tail-32 spans **1**; Ming Chen 229 across 4, tail-32 spans 2. Each tail-32 centroid reproduces that Speaker's stored Voiceprint at `1.000000`, so these are the rows the product minted from and not a reconstruction. **Three findings that cut against assuming the fix is free, and they are the reason this is a spec rather than a change.** First, on this History spreading prevents nothing: tail, spread and all-rows centroids each match nobody else in the gallery at `MATCH_FLOOR`, so for these five the tail is narrow but not wrong. Second, spreading **lowers** mean pairwise coherence in every case — Jack Ahn 0.6994 → 0.6214, Ming Chen 0.6143 → 0.5846, Hong Li 0.6641 → 0.6391, Marc Ammann 0.6866 → 0.6825 — because breadth means different rooms, microphones and distances; a wider sample is a more honest picture and a noisier one. Third, and the part that bears on the user's pending choice: the weakest legitimate control on mean pairwise is the 229-exemplar `Ming Chen` row at **0.6143**, which *is* ticket 13's upper band bound, and spreading drops it to **0.5846**. A guard tuned before this change starts refusing a legitimate Speaker after it. **Whichever of 13 and 14 lands second must re-measure the band**, and picking 14's selection rule first costs nothing and settles the numbers 13's threshold is chosen against. **The case where the tail did real harm recurs, and this is established from the real History rather than from the deleted copy.** Q248's wrong Voiceprint was minted entirely from the tail of `01a08e16`. That 483-exemplar state lived on a throwaway copy that is gone — but exemplars are minted one per attributed segment in chronological order, and the real History today attributes the Operator **483 segments across 6 Meetings whose last 32 fall in `01a08e16` alone**. A relearn run now would concentrate on the same Meeting again. **Stated limitation:** spreading alone would not have prevented that failure, since every window in that tail carried double-talk; a six-Meeting sample would have been cleaner, not clean. 14 does not substitute for 13. **Nothing was built and nothing on the host changed** — `/Applications`, TCC and the real History untouched, the helper script removed afterwards.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** c0851a0

## Q252 — issues/13 — tradeoff

**Question:** A regression test for the mint defect has to keep `main` green while a known defect is unfixed. Ignored-with-reason, or a characterization test pinning today's behaviour?
**Options considered:** `#[ignore]` with a reason / characterization tests written to flip / both
**Chosen:** **Two characterization tests**, `today_a_centroid_is_minted_from_exemplars_that_disagree_completely` and `today_the_cap_takes_a_contiguous_tail_rather_than_a_sample`, each naming its ticket and stating in its doc comment exactly what to replace the body with when the guard or the spread lands.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The user offered either shape and left the choice open. Characterization was taken because an `#[ignore]`d test runs nowhere and rots: it asserts behaviour that does not exist, nothing checks it against the code, and its failure when the guard lands is invisible because it never ran. A characterization test runs on every commit, so it is checked continuously against the real function, and it fails loudly the moment the behaviour changes — which is the signal wanted, since the change will be deliberate. The cost is that a passing test now describes a defect, which is why both are prefixed `today_` and both doc comments open *"Characterization, not approval"*. **The first test corrected my own framing, which is worth recording.** I first wrote it to assert that a blend of two orthogonal voices matches neither above `MATCH_FLOOR`. It failed: two orthogonal unit vectors average to a vector at 0.7071 to each, comfortably *above* the 0.62 floor. The defect is not that any disagreement produces a vector matching nothing — it is that the **split** decides who keeps the name. Orthogonal groups of size m and n leave the smaller at `m/sqrt(m² + n²)`, which crosses under the floor once the minority is below about four fifths of the majority; the real History's 4/5 split left the Operator's own exemplars at min 0.5142. The test now uses 3/6 and asserts the minority falls below the floor while the majority stays above it, which is the property that generalises rather than a number that happened to hold. **Green verified:** 64 cluster tests pass, `cargo fmt --check` clean, and clippy is back to the one pre-existing `chunks_exact` warning in `server.rs:4998` that was there before this change — the two `repeat().take()` warnings these tests introduced were fixed with `repeat_n`.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** c0851a0

## Q253 — issues/03 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Ticket 03 left one finding open: the segmentation slide cuts DER through falling confusion rather than placement, at 9.3× the inference, and *why* confusion falls was untested. Why does it fall?
**Options considered:** offset aliasing (a 10 s grid cutting turns badly) / evidence quantity (more windows per speaker, tighter centroids)
**Chosen:** **Not alignment.** Ten alignments at the production step, window count held identical, spread the covered-confusion rate by 0.60 points (sd 0.18) and recover **5%** of the 5.45-point fall a finer step produces. Aliasing is refuted directly; observation count is what remains. Two further results: the fall **saturates at a 2 s step**, so 5× the inference buys everything 9.3× does, and a 5 s step is **pathological**, 4.24 points worse than 10 s. `SEGMENT_STEP` stays `SEGMENT_WINDOW` — the verdict is unchanged.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Asked for by the user. Run on the *current* shipping configuration — ReDimNet2-B3 since Q226, all 16 AMI meetings, nothing reaching the network (ADR-0002) — so the absolute numbers are not the WeSpeaker-era 29.85/23.88 the finding was written against and are not presented as reproducing them. **The characterization itself reproduces.** 10 s → 1 s is −4.31 DER (24.60 → 20.29), carried by confusion (−5.02) while the oracle floor moves −0.71: the gain is in labelling, not placement, as the finding said. **A correction the user raised and the numbers bear out.** Missed rose 10.40 → 12.96 at 2 s while confusion fell, and speech the hypothesis does not cover cannot be scored as confused, so part of the fall could have been coverage lost rather than labelling won. Re-expressed on covered speech — `covered = total − missed` exactly, from the scorer's own `saturating_sub` identity — confusion falls 11.12% → 9.13% → 5.66% → 5.59%, still monotone. Of the raw 5.03-point fall to 2 s, **4.89 survives** a proportional correction and **at least 2.47 survives** a bound that assumes nothing at all: even if every millisecond newly missed had been fully confused before, it can only account for 2.56. The proportional figure does assume the newly-missed speech is of average difficulty; the stricter common-support version needs region-level output the harness does not print, and the bound makes the conclusion safe without it. **The mechanism needed an experiment the step sweep cannot provide,** because shrinking the step multiplies the windows *and* moves them. The knob added for it is `LiveDiarizer::with_phase(phase_ms)`, mirroring the existing `with_step`, changing the walk to `(self.phase..samples.len()).step_by(self.step)`, harness-only with production defaulting to 0 — and the harness's snapshot provenance stamp now carries `phase_ms`, without which one phase's cached inference would have been served to another and the whole sweep would have been ten identical runs. Phase 0 was re-run as a control and reproduced the baseline in every figure (DER 24.60, missed 10.40, false alarm 4.24, confusion 9.96, floor 19.68), which is what licenses reading the other nine. **The shape was genuinely ambiguous before it,** which is why it was worth the 95 minutes: only 36% of the fall arrives at the first sub-window step, too little for aliasing, yet 1 s adds nothing over 2 s, too flat for quantity alone. The phase sweep breaks the tie one way. **Stated honestly: aliasing is refuted directly, quantity is the survivor by elimination plus the monotone step trend, not proven independently.** The saturation at 2 s is a diminishing return, so "quantity" means enough observations rather than more without limit. **The 5 s result is worth recording because it is a trap.** DER 28.84 against 10 s's 24.60, on a false-alarm rise of +9.90 with the oracle floor flat — so it is not extra speech being detected but one speaker fragmented across clusters that are active at the same moment, which the scorer charges as extra speakers. Anyone sampling two step values could land on it and conclude the slide is harmful. **What this does not change:** `SEGMENT_STEP` stays `SEGMENT_WINDOW`. The plan fixed a 2-point oracle-floor bar in advance and the floor moves 0.71 here, so the criterion is no more met than before. Adoption remains the user's. **Green:** `cargo fmt --check` clean, clippy at the one pre-existing `chunks_exact` warning, 669 of 670 core lib tests passing — the one failure is `detect::macos::a_real_microphone_hold_is_visible_to_the_detector`, which needs a microphone TCC grant this machine does not give the test binary and is unrelated to this change.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 4ebc5f3

## Q254 — issues/14 — deviation

**Question:** Ticket 14's selection rule was chosen by the user: round-robin across contributing Meetings, newest Meeting first, newest-first within each, until `MAX_EXEMPLARS`. Where does that selection live, and does the spread keep ticket 13's band admissible on the real History?
**Options considered:** teach `centroid` the round-robin (one function, but it has a second caller for which a Meeting round-robin is meaningless) / select at the mint site and leave `centroid` as the averaging primitive
**Chosen:** **A new `cluster::spread_across_meetings(&[Option<&str>]) -> Vec<usize>` called from `refresh_voiceprint` only.** `centroid`'s signature is unchanged. The re-measured band is **(0.3190, 0.5867)** and the predeclared threshold 0.50 sits inside it with 0.0867 of headroom above the weakest control.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The rule was the user's; only its placement and the re-measurement were open. **Placement.** `centroid` has exactly two production callers: this mint (`cluster.rs`, multi-Meeting) and `live.rs:850`, which averages one run's observations — all from a single Meeting, where a Meeting round-robin is a no-op at best and misleading at worst. `centroid`'s parameter is `&[(Vec<f32>, i64, bool)]` and carries no Meeting id, whereas `Exemplar` does. Selecting at the mint keeps one concept per function: the mint decides *which* exemplars, `centroid` decides *how* to average them. `centroid`'s own `.rev().take(MAX_EXEMPLARS)` is left in place and is now a no-op for this caller, retained as the cost bound for the single-Meeting one. **The selection pre-filters.** Negatives, empty vectors and out-of-space rows are dropped *before* the cap is spent, not after — otherwise a negative would consume one of the 32 slots and then be discarded by `centroid`, which is the same silent narrowing the ticket is about. On this History the pre-filter is a no-op (0 negatives, 0 empty vectors) but it is the correct order regardless. **No new constants; the cap stays 32**, as asked. **A deviation from the user's literal instruction, and why.** I was asked to "flip `today_the_cap_takes_a_contiguous_tail_rather_than_a_sample` into its real assertion". That test targets `centroid` directly, and `centroid`'s tail-take is deliberately unchanged, so flipping it in place would have asserted the fix of a function that does not have it. It is **retargeted** rather than edited: renamed `the_cap_is_spread_across_meetings_rather_than_spent_on_a_tail`, it now asserts the selection's share (5 Meetings × 40 exemplars yields shares of 6 or 7 each, never 32 and four zeroes), that the newest row within a Meeting is taken and the oldest is not, and that the newest Meeting leads. The test that pinned the defect now pins the fix, which is what the instruction was for. Two more cover the edges the spread must not break: a Speaker heard once still fills the cap newest-first (`chosen[0] == 99`, `chosen[31] == 68`), unattributed exemplars fall in one bucket rather than minting less, and under the cap every exemplar survives. One db-backed test runs the whole mint path — two Meetings, forty exemplars each, the voice reading differently in the second — and asserts the stored Voiceprint is above 0.6 to *both*; under the old tail it was exactly 0.0 to the earlier one. **The re-measurement, read-only, `mode=ro`, real History, nothing written.** The band's lower bound is unmoved: the Operator has 9 usable exemplars over 6 Meetings, under the cap, so the selection is a no-op for them and they stay at 0.3190. The upper bound **fell**, from 0.6105 to 0.5867 — the weakest named control is the 230-exemplar `Ming Chen`, now drawing on 5 Meetings instead of 3. That is the narrowing Q251 predicted and it is the real cost of the spread: breadth means different rooms and microphones, so within-speaker coherence drops. Three Speakers changed at all (Jack Ahn 3 → 9 Meetings and 0.7136 → 0.6316, Hong Li 2 → 5 and 0.6515 → 0.6545, Ming Chen 3 → 5 and 0.6105 → 0.5867); the other 24 are under the cap and untouched. 0.50 survives with 0.0867 of headroom, down from 0.1105 — narrower, still admissible, and still predeclared before ticket 13 ships. **The History has moved since Q251's table was written and the report says so rather than papering over it.** It now holds 12 Meetings, not 10: two were recorded at 17:32 today, which is why Jack Ahn reads 890 usable over 9 Meetings against Q251's 888 over 7. Both columns of the before/after comparison come from one read of the current database, so the old-vs-new figures are directly comparable; it is Q251's absolute numbers that are now stale, not this comparison. **The measuring script is a verbatim port of the shipped Rust**, and was checked against all four of the Rust unit tests' cases before its output was trusted — the band depends on the port being faithful, so agreement was verified rather than assumed. It ran read-only and was deleted from the host afterwards. **Green:** `cargo fmt --check` clean, clippy at the one pre-existing `chunks_exact` warning in `server.rs`, 672 of 673 core lib tests passing — the one failure is `detect::macos::a_real_microphone_hold_is_visible_to_the_detector`, which needs a microphone TCC grant this machine does not give the test binary and is unrelated.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 1be7efc

## Q255 — issues/13 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Ticket 13's guard was specified in full — measure, action, threshold — with AMI as the gate it had to clear before shipping. It does not clear it. Does the guard ship anyway, at a moved threshold, or not at all?
**Options considered:** ship 0.50 on the specified measure / move the threshold into the surviving window / change the aggregation and keep 0.50 / do not ship the refusal
**Chosen:** **—** The refusal is **not shipped.** The measure, the candidate threshold and the whole validation land as code and evidence; which measure the guard reads is escalated, because no threshold move rescues the one specified and the alternative that works is a change of measure rather than of threshold.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Built as specified first: `agreement`, mean pairwise cosine over exactly the post-14 selected exemplars, `None` under two; the refusal folded into the existing `clear_voiceprint` fall-through with a `tracing::warn!` naming Speaker, score, floor and count; no protocol surface. Then measured, and the measurement withdrew it. **The AMI gate, and it fails.** AMI carries global speaker ids — sixteen real people, each heard in four of the sixteen meetings — so the round-robin is genuinely exercised. One pass at production settings (step 10000 ms, phase 0, ReDimNet2-B3, 589.7 s of inference over 32623.9 s of audio, nothing from the network) produced 5644 observations, each attributed to the reference speaker whose speech it covers. Scored in the shape the guard actually binds on — one exemplar per attributed range, which is what `reseed.rs` writes and what **every** over-cap Speaker on the real History is made of (20–99 rows per Meeting, all carrying sample windows, confirmed read-only) — **seven of the sixteen fall under 0.50**: FEO070 0.3759, MTD0010ID 0.4153, FIO084 0.4192, MEE071 0.4458, MEE073 0.4644, FIO089 0.4731, MTD012ME 0.4938. Attribute windows without requiring them to be single-voice and all sixteen fall, down to 0.2081. Every one is one person. **No threshold move rescues it, which is why this is escalated rather than journalled as a move.** The user's instruction anticipated a move; the distribution says there is nowhere to move to. A threshold must sit above the contaminated Operator's 0.3190 and below the weakest legitimate speaker: 0.3759 strict leaves a window **0.057** wide, and 0.2081 permissive leaves **none at all** — the legitimate speaker scores *below* the contamination. Mean pairwise over raw exemplars measures how hard the audio is at least as much as whether the evidence is one voice, and AMI's far-field meeting rooms are harder than the calls this product records. The real History's six controls did not show that because they are all clean-channel; six controls on one History is exactly the overfitting the AMI gate existed to catch, and it caught it. **One of three aggregations does separate, on both corpora, at the user's own 0.50.** Averaging each Meeting's selected exemplars before comparing them: AMI's weakest real person 0.6813 strict / 0.6767 permissive, the Operator 0.3597, the weakest real-History control 0.7474 — a joint window of **(0.3597, 0.6767)**, 0.317 wide, with 0.50 inside it by 0.140 and 0.177. Minimum pairwise is dead: AMI's legitimate speakers reach −0.1740, below the Operator's −0.1216, so its window is empty too. **Why the surviving option is not mine to take.** It is a change of measure, and it has its own blind spot: contamination that is uniform across Meetings blends every Meeting's centroid the same way, so the centroids agree and it passes — arguably the *likelier* real case, one colleague leaking into every call. **A second blind spot, in the specified measure, found on the synthetic mixes and pinned as a test.** Two orthogonal voices in an `m:n` split score `[C(m,2)+C(n,2)]/C(m+n,2)`, which **rises** as the split gets more lopsided while the minority's cosine to the blend falls: 4:5 scores 0.4444 and is refused, and the minority would have kept its name at 0.6247; 1:8 scores 0.7778 and passes, and the Voiceprint is essentially the other person's voice. The guard refuses the harmless split and passes the harmful ones, and it cannot be tuned out — 2:7 scores 0.6111, above the 0.5867 the weakest real control scores. The Q252 split, 3:6, lands exactly on 0.5000 and so passes by the strict comparison. **What landed.** `agreement` as a `pub` measurement primitive beside `cosine`; `AGREEMENT_FLOOR` as a documented candidate that nothing reads; five tests — the measure separating the 4/5 mix, one voice recorded badly not being refused, too-little-evidence exempt, the dominant-contamination blind spot, and a db-backed characterization pinning that a contaminated Speaker **still** gets a Voiceprint today, written to fail when the guard is adopted. `refresh_voiceprint` is byte-for-byte unchanged in behaviour from before this entry. **A correction to my own ticket-14 fixture, caught by the guard while it was briefly active.** It used two orthogonal voices across two Meetings to prove the spread works; that is a 16:16 split scoring 0.4839 and the guard refused it, correctly — a voice is not orthogonal to itself. The fixture is now the same voice in two rooms at cosine 0.55, which the spread lifts to 0.88 against both readings where the tail left 0.55. **Green:** `cargo fmt --check` clean, clippy at the one pre-existing `chunks_exact` warning, 676 of 677 core lib tests passing plus `meeting_lifecycle` 10, `diarize_queue` 11, `protocol_contract` 9 and the protocol crate's 111 — the one failure is the microphone-TCC-gated `detect::macos` test, unrelated.
**Outcome:** escalated
**Ref:** 4e47e4b

## Q256 — issues/13 — gate-resolution

**Question:** The third measure for ticket 13's guard — a two-way partition test, specified in full by the user with both numbers predeclared — passes five of the six validation criteria they set. The sixth fails. Does it ship?
**Options considered:** ship on the five it passes / move a number to clear the sixth / judge the sixth criterion unrepresentative and ship / do not ship
**Chosen:** **—** The refusal is **not shipped**, and neither number is moved. `split`, `Split::is_two_voices`, `AGREEMENT_FLOOR` and `MINORITY_SHARE` land as measurement surface with the full distribution recorded; `refresh_voiceprint` is unchanged in behaviour. Whether the failing criterion should bind is escalated.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Built exactly as specified: two-means over the post-14 selected exemplars, seeded from the least-similar pair, assigned to the nearer group centroid, iterated to a fixed point; refuse when the two group centroids score under 0.50 **and** the minority holds at least a quarter of the exemplars and at least two. Refuse, never subset, for the reason the user gave — on the Operator's own record the majority group is the contaminant, so a dominant subset mints the wrong voice with more confidence. **Five criteria pass.** The Operator's real 9, read-only: refused, group centroids at **−0.0923** with a minority of 4 of 9. All six named real-History controls mint. Synthetic 4:5 and 3:6: refused, centroids 0.0000, minorities 4 and 3. Synthetic 2:7 and 1:8: pass by design. AMI under strict attribution: **0 refused of 16**, both exemplar shapes. **The sixth fails.** With exemplars cut from whole transcript segments and windows attributed to whoever dominates them, **10 of AMI's 16 real people are refused** — FIO084 at 0.0422 with a minority of 13 of 32, FEO072 0.0706/11, MEE014 0.1136/16, MEO015 0.1512/10, MEE073 0.1636/15, FIO087 0.1801/12, MTD011UID 0.2352/15, FEO070 0.2531/9, MEE071 0.2562/10, FIO089 0.2888/15. **The measure works as designed; the argument is about what it was fed.** It separates on the real History by a wide margin and it separates AMI's strictly-attributed pools perfectly, so the partition is not the problem. Under permissive attribution the *pool* holds two voices: the refused speakers' minority groups have mean purity 0.629 against the reference annotation, and 113 of their 126 exemplars are windows where the filed speaker held under 90% of the audio. **I checked whether that lets the failure be dismissed, and it does not.** Both halves are impure — majority groups run 0.525 to 0.777, no cleaner than the minorities — so the partition is not finding "speaker versus contaminant", it is cutting a uniformly mixed pool in half. More decisively, **permissive attribution is the closer model of production, not the unfair one.** `reseed::plan` builds each range as `Sample { channel, start_ms, end_ms }` straight from `transcript_segments` (reseed.rs:189), one whole segment per exemplar, with no clean-runs filter and no overlap exclusion — and the reseed path is what every over-cap Speaker on the real History is made of. Strict attribution, which requires a window to be 90% one voice, is the *optimistic* reading. So the criterion the user predeclared is the one that models the shipping path, and it is the one that fails. **What the real History does and does not license.** Its six controls all mint, but each of three is held by only one of the two conditions: Jack Ahn's centroids agree at 0.5229, 0.023 above the floor, and are saved by a minority of 2 of 32; the 79-exemplar `Ming Chen` has a minority of 13 of 32, over the quarter, and is saved by centroids at 0.9650; the 230-exemplar `Ming Chen` has centroids at **0.2865**, well under the floor, and is saved by a minority of 1 of 32. The conjunction is doing real work rather than sitting idle — which is the design behaving as intended, and also means the product's own audio already produces named Speakers that one condition alone would refuse. Six controls on one History cannot settle whether AMI's overlap rate or this History's is the better guide to what users will record. **The quarter, journalled as the accepted blind spot the user asked for.** A record dominated three to one mints its dominant voice whatever the minority is; whether that voice is the named person is attribution's problem, not agreement's. On this History it is load-bearing, not theoretical: it is the only thing keeping the 230-exemplar `Ming Chen`'s Voiceprint. **Why this is escalated rather than decided.** The remaining question is which corpus's overlap rate represents the product's users — AMI's far-field meeting rooms, or this History's channel-separated calls. That is a judgement about who uses this and how, of the same kind as `MATCH_FLOOR`, and the user's own instruction for this case was to report and escalate without moving either number. Shipping on my reading of the purity data would be deciding it by assertion, having been told not to. **What landed.** `split` and `Split` as `pub` measurement surface beside `cosine`; both constants documented with what the gate said; five tests — 4:5 and 3:6 read as two voices, one voice recorded in two rooms not read as two (the case both earlier measures failed, its raw worst pair under the floor and its partitioned centroids above it), 2:7 and 1:8 not read as two by the quarter, the edges, and a db-backed characterization pinning that a contaminated Speaker **still** gets a Voiceprint, written to fail when the guard is adopted. **The ports were verified, not assumed**: the Python used for both corpora reproduces the Rust's verdict on every predeclared synthetic case and on the edges before it was pointed at real data. **Green:** `cargo fmt --check` clean, clippy at the one pre-existing `chunks_exact` warning, 676 of 677 core lib tests plus `meeting_lifecycle` 10, `diarize_queue` 11, `protocol_contract` 9 — the failure is the microphone-TCC-gated `detect::macos` test, unrelated.
**Outcome:** escalated
**Ref:** fa7de85

## Q257 — issues/15 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Ticket 15 was specified conditionally: drop an exemplar window that the other channel talked over, "covering the machine path too if it selects windows the same way". Does it?
**Options considered:** apply the drop to both write paths' exemplars / apply it to the reseed exemplar and to the machine path's observations / apply it to the reseed path only and leave the machine path unaddressed
**Chosen:** The reseed path takes the rule at the exemplar, because its vector *is* the window's embedding. The machine path does **not** select windows the same way, so the rule is scoped one level up, to the observations before the cluster centroid consumes them — and the distinction is written into the ticket's acceptance criteria so it is not later "simplified" into an exemplar-level check. Ticket 15 is specified and measured read-only; nothing is built.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **Why the condition resolves negatively.** `reseed.rs:389` writes one exemplar per attributed range and the vector is that one whole `transcript_segments` row embedded, so its `sample_*` columns describe the audio the vector came from and the drop rule is exact. `cluster.rs:1123` writes one exemplar per Speaker per Meeting whose vector is `centroid` over *every* observation of that voice in the run; its `sample` is set from the middle of the longest **clean** run (`live.rs:858`) and is a playback pointer chosen for listening. Dropping that exemplar because its pointer overlaps would discard a whole run's evidence on the strength of a window that did not produce the vector — the opposite of the ticket's intent. Each `Observation` does carry its own `channel` and `runs` on the capture clock (`live.rs:288–307`), so the filter is expressible there, at `live.rs:865` where `grouped` is assembled. Worth recording that this path already computes `clean_runs` — "the stretches it was the only voice" — and still feeds the unfiltered `observation.vector` to the centroid; and that `clean_runs` is **same-channel only**, so it says nothing about the far end and is not a partial version of this rule. No exemplar in the real History is machine-shaped today (every usable row carries a sample window and the counts match the reseed writes), so that half is specified and unmeasured, which the ticket says. **Measured before building, read-only, `mode=ro`, nothing written, as instructed.** The port was verified first: the recomputed centroid reproduces the **stored** Voiceprint at 1.00000 for every Speaker at or under the cap and for both single-Meeting Speakers over it, and the four that differ (Hong Li 0.94280, `Ming Chen` 230 0.98900, Marc Ammann 0.99638, Jack Ahn 0.99944) are exactly the multi-Meeting over-cap rows whose Voiceprints predate ticket 14 — ticket 14's effect measured a second way, not a port error. The `spread_across_meetings` port was separately checked against all three Rust unit cases before use. **The finding that makes this the right ticket:** the rule **repairs** the Operator's record where ticket 13 could at best refuse it. All nine of their usable exemplars are on mic; six overlap system speech and go; the surviving three score **0.9811** against the Operator's own clean voice `d859b1` where the blend of all nine sat at **0.6183**, with the far-end `Ming Chen` rows unmoved at 0.3283 and 0.3164. The two centroids agree with each other at only 0.6227, so this is a different vector rather than a rounding of the same one. **The cost, reported and not softened.** Five of six named controls keep their identity — survivors against the Voiceprint each carries today: Hong Li 48/153 at 0.9443, Jack Ahn 160/890 at 0.9713, Marc Ammann 29/59 at 0.9827, `Ming Chen` 36/79 at 0.9701, `Ming Chen` 70/230 at 0.8654. **`Menggang Xu` is stranded**: one exemplar, on mic, overlapped, so every usable row goes and `refresh_voiceprint` falls through to `clear_voiceprint`. Reported rather than accommodated, as instructed. What bounds it: clearing sets no forgotten mark, so the name and the identity survive and only recognition is lost until that voice is heard again. Across every Speaker, 1472 usable exemplars, **1115 dropped (76%)**, 15 left with none — that one named Speaker and fourteen pseudonyms. The 76% is measured, not endorsed; Q258 is what decides whether it is affordable. **Ticket 13 is also settled by this entry.** The user ruled AMI's overlap rate representative and deferred the guard, so nothing from 13 ships and its three withdrawn measures stay as measurement surface. Its ticket file gains the second blind spot the purity check exposed: because both halves of every refused AMI pool are impure (0.629 minority against 0.525–0.777 majority), the partition test cannot distinguish one voice recorded with varied contamination from two voices — a Speaker heard across several rooms with a different far end leaking into each scores like a record holding two people. That is distinct from the accepted quarter blind spot, which is about a minority too small to notice; this is about the test being unable to say *what* its two groups are. Refusing stays the safe act, but a refusal is not evidence a second person is present and must not be surfaced as though it were. **Decision (3) is dropped on the user's instruction** — with no guard adopted and the Operator holding no Voiceprint, a walk on `macbook-pro-nickel` would only re-show Q248.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 3ed621c

## Q258 — issues/15 — escalated

**Question:** The drop rule as specified is symmetric — any opposite-channel speech drops the window — but the two channels have different physical contamination paths. Should the rule stay symmetric?
**Options considered:** keep it symmetric as specified / drop only mic windows overlapped by system speech / make it a tunable proportion
**Chosen:** **—** The ticket is specified with the symmetric rule the user gave. The one-directional variant is measured and reported beside it, and neither is adopted.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **The asymmetry is physical, not a threshold.** The mic recording can contain far-end speech, because the speakers leak into the microphone — the path Q246 localised. The system recording is a tap of the application's output stream, so the Operator's own voice has no route into it. The symmetric rule treats both directions as contaminating; only one of them is. **Measured, so the question is quantified rather than argued.** The Operator's repair is **bit-for-bit identical** under both rules — 3 of 9 kept, 0.9811 against their own clean voice — because every one of their exemplars is on mic. The six named controls hold 1372 of 1412 exemplars on the **system** channel, so under the mic-only variant they keep 153/153, 866/890, 58/59, 79/79 and 230/230, against today's Voiceprint at 0.9428, 0.9994, 0.9960, 1.0000 and 0.9890. That is a drop rate near 2% against the symmetric rule's 76%, for the same repair. The stranding is not caused by the symmetric direction either: `Menggang Xu`'s single exemplar is on mic and is dropped under both. **Why this is not mine to take, and why it is not a softening.** The user's instruction was explicit that the rule carries no threshold and that a stranded named Speaker is to be reported rather than used as grounds to weaken it, and I have not weakened it — the ticket is written with the symmetric rule and the stranding recorded as expected. But symmetric-versus-directional is not a threshold at all; it is a claim about which capture paths can carry contamination, and a wrong answer either throws away three quarters of the evidence for nothing or leaves a real leak in place. That is a judgement about the product's audio of the same kind as `MATCH_FLOOR`, and the 74-point difference in drop rate is large enough that assuming it silently would decide the ticket's cost by assertion. Both readings are in the ticket file with their numbers.
**Outcome:** escalated
**Ref:** 3ed621c

## Q259 — issues/15 — gate-resolution

**Question:** The installed app on `macbook-pro-nickel` holds the real History and recorded two Meetings today, and NOTES said the installed v1.1.1 "must not start" because it would read ReDimNet2's 18 045 013 bytes as `Corrupted`, re-fetch WeSpeaker over it, and let Q227's lazy path rebuild every Voiceprint into the old space. It started. Did the hazard fire, and are Q249–Q258's real-History numbers therefore worthless?
**Options considered:** the hazard fired and the record is in WeSpeaker or mixed space — discard the affected numbers and stop the copy walk / it did not fire — say why the build tolerated the file and correct the NOTES claim
**Chosen:** **It did not fire.** The record is uniformly ReDimNet2, Q249–Q258's real-History numbers stand, and the copy walk's input is correct, so it continues. The NOTES claim is corrected: the installed bundle is a build from `main`, and the version string never distinguished it from the dangerous one.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **Four checks, all read-only, real History and `/Applications` untouched.** (1) `diarize-embedding.onnx` is 18 045 013 bytes with sha256 `dcecdce7d52bbd4739b24d0874359ec564d43f4b3a392f0104f505593b566d41` — an exact match to the `redimnet2-b3-vox2-lm` entry at `registry.rs:281` — mtime 09-17 04:57, unmodified; `diarize-embedding-wespeaker.onnx` is 26 535 549 / `3955447b…`, mtime 09-05 07:26, untouched. (2) All **26** stored Voiceprints and all **1472** exemplars are stamped `redimnet2-b3`/`1`; there are no WeSpeaker rows and no mixed space. Both Meetings recorded after the app started — `01a0b184` at 15:37:21 and `01a0b1ed` at 17:32:24 — carry 7 exemplars each, same stamp. (3) Registry `diarize_rerun` reads `(1, 'redimnet2-b3', '1', 10, 0, 0, '2026-09-17T04:58:49.174474-07:00')`, i.e. still the 04:58 walk over 10 Meetings, with nothing since. (4) No fetch: the models directory holds exactly five files with no partial downloads, four dated 09-05 and the embedding 09-17 04:57, and `log show --predicate 'process == "evertranscript"' --start "2026-09-17 15:30:00"` returns **zero** lines matching `corrupt|fetch|download`. **Why the build tolerated the file — the part NOTES got wrong.** The bundle does report `CFBundleShortVersionString` `1.1.1`, but its binary was replaced: the bundle's mtime is 2026-09-17 15:35:52 and `Contents/Resources/evertranscript` is 15:37:07, and `strings` on that binary yields `redimnet2-b3` **19** times against `wespeaker-voxceleb-resnet34-LM` once. It is Q235's attended install of a build from `main`, whose registry expects exactly those 18 045 013 bytes at that filename — so the file is correct for it, not corrupt. The first Meeting was recorded at 15:37:21, fourteen seconds after the binary landed. **The flaw in "must not start" is that it identified a build by its version string, and `Cargo.toml:16` puts `main` at `1.1.1` too** — the same version named both the dangerous bundle and its replacement, so the warning could not have told them apart and would have kept warning after the hazard was gone. NOTES now identifies the build by what it links, records the install and the four checks, and notes that the Q241 login-item re-enable is unblocked but not done and is the user's call.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 4f26667

## Q260 — issues/15 — deviation

**Question:** Verifying the two post-install Meetings showed exemplars with no matching transcript segment, which contradicts Q257's premise that no exemplar in the real History is machine-shaped. What is the true population, and what does it cost the measurement Q257 reported?
**Options considered:** treat it as a detail of two new Meetings and leave the measurement as reported / re-census by writer and correct whatever the wrong premise carried
**Chosen:** Re-censused. **64 of 1472 rows are machine-shaped, including all nine of the Operator's**, so Q257's Operator measurement tested a coarse per-Meeting proxy rather than the specified per-window rule. Ticket 15 and the pending rebuild measurement are corrected; the shipped code is unchanged and the rebuild becomes the first real test of the machine-path filter.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **The exact discriminator is the writer's own tell, not a span join.** A reseed row's vector *is* its window, so `voiced_ms == sample_end_ms - sample_start_ms`; a machine row's vector is the cluster centroid against a 10s playback pointer, so the two disagree. By that test: reseed 1407 named/Operator + 1 pseudonym; **machine 14 named/Operator over 8 Meetings + 50 pseudonym**. The 14 are the Operator's nine, all on mic, plus five on system for `Jack Ahn` ×2, `Hong Li`, `Marc Ammann` and `Ming Chen`. Five came from today's two Meetings and the rest from live diarization after the 04:58 bulk re-run, which reseeds the relearnable Speakers and leaves the newly recognised ones to the machine path. The error was reading `named` as a synonym for `reseed-shaped` — true of 1407 rows, not all of them. **What it costs.** Q257's Operator result (0.6183 → **0.9811** on 3 of 9 kept) is arithmetically sound — those are the nine stored vectors and that is the centroid of the three — but the selector was a playback pointer from the middle of the longest *same-channel* clean run, which did not produce the vector. So it dropped whole mic clusters by where their pointer landed: a coarse per-Meeting selector, not the per-window rule. It works because a pointer drawn from a clean run in a Meeting full of far-end speech is itself likely to sit in far-end speech, so it proxies for a contaminated cluster. **This is precisely the mistake Q257's own justification argued against** — "dropping that exemplar because its pointer overlaps would discard a whole run's evidence on the strength of a window that did not produce the vector" — and then asserted could not arise. **What is unaffected:** the six named controls' numbers, because their five machine rows are all on system and mic-only never drops a system row; `Menggang Xu`'s stranding, whose single row is reseed-shaped and on mic; and the shipped filters, since Q257 already scoped the machine path to the observations before `centroid` for exactly this reason. **Not a supersede.** Q257's `Chosen` — exemplar-level for reseed, observation-level for the machine path — is unchanged and is what the correction vindicates; only a factual premise in its justification was wrong, and the journal is append-only, so this entry is the correction rather than an edit. **What improves:** the machine-path change is no longer unmeasurable here. The record keeps only the centroid, so no stored row can show an observation filter working — but the Operator's nine rows are all machine-written, which makes the Operator's after-vs-before cosine in tonight's rebuild a direct reading of it. The pending measurement script was rewritten before the walk landed to partition by the writer tell and to state that a machine row's pointer overlapping the far end is not a violation.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 4f26667

## Q261 — issues/15 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Does ticket 15's mic-only filter do on the real record what it was built to do — and at what cost?
**Options considered:** read the rebuilt copy against the untouched real History / accept the pre-build projection from Q257
**Chosen:** Measured. **The rule holds exactly** (27 → **0** violating rows) and **the Operator gains a Voiceprint at 0.9960** against their own clean voice where they had none. The cost is pseudonym recognition: 373 segments leave pseudonyms, 264 of them becoming unattributed, and 9 of 20 pseudonyms lose their vector. `Menggang Xu`'s predicted stranding happened. Nothing is adopted by this entry — it reports what the built filter does.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **Isolation first.** The real History's sha256 is `0f010a302c0e1e6e…`, byte-identical to the pre-walk snapshot; the bulk re-run ran against `/tmp/et-t15/history` under the Q243 env isolation, PID 84192, while the installed app's Core held the real one at PID 70215. 12 of 12 Meetings walked, 0 abandoned. **The rule is exact on the path that ships it.** Partitioning by the writer's own tell (Q260), reseed-shaped mic rows overlapping far-end speech go from **27 of 27** before to **0 of 133** after — every enrolled mic window that the other channel talked over is gone, and the population grew rather than shrank. System rows are untouched by design: 1039/1381 → 1214/1608 still overlapped, which is the mic-only asymmetry (Q258) behaving as chosen. The 3 of 8 machine-shaped mic rows whose *pointer* overlaps are not violations, per Q260. **The Operator is repaired, and better than projected.** Before: no Voiceprint at all, 9 machine-written exemplars. After: **139 exemplars** — 133 reseed-written over 5 Meetings plus 6 machine-written over 6 — and a Voiceprint scoring **0.9960** against their own clean voice `d859b1`, with the far-end `Ming Chen` rows at 0.3365 and 0.3110. Q257 projected 0.9811 from the stored vectors; the real rebuild reaches 0.9960, because the reseed path enrols filtered windows rather than reusing contaminated cluster centroids. Attribution rises from 483 segments over 6 Meetings to **591 over all 12** — the Operator is now attributed in every Meeting, gaining `01a0a295`, `01a0a7b9`, `01a0a7da`, `01a0ac58`, `01a0b184`, `01a0b1ed` and losing none. This is the Q234/Q236 regression shape resolving in the right direction, and it is the first real reading of the machine-path observation filter, since all nine of the Operator's prior rows were machine-written. **The named controls keep their identities.** Hong Li 153→172 at 0.9536, Jack Ahn 890→1031 at 0.9673 (its 24 mic rows all dropped, 24→0), Marc Ammann 59→69 at 0.9953, `Ming Chen` 79→79 at 1.0000, `Ming Chen` 230→257 at 0.9740. Named-with-a-vector is 6 before and 6 after, but the membership trades: **the Operator gains recognition and `Menggang Xu` loses it.** **The stranding happened and is bounded as claimed.** `Menggang Xu` held one exemplar, on mic, overlapped; it is dropped, they end with 0 and no vector — and `forgotten = 0`, so the name and the identity survive and their one attributed segment is still theirs. Reported, not softened, as instructed. `Yaning Liu` also has no vector, but held 0 exemplars before too — pre-existing, not caused by the rule. **The cost, stated plainly.** Segments by owner move from unattributed 1265 / operator 483 / named 1644 / pseudonym 739 to unattributed **1529** / operator 591 / named 1645 / pseudonym **366**. So 373 segments leave pseudonyms: 108 go to the Operator, who is now correctly recognised, and **264 become unattributed** — 6.4% of all 4131 segments show as unassigned rather than as a numbered Speaker. Pseudonyms with a vector fall 20 → 11. That is the documented handled state (`cluster.rs:1078-1081`: a cluster with no embedding is left out of `embeddings` while its turns are kept), not a new failure mode, and it costs a placeholder label rather than a wrong name. **The confound, not hidden:** a bulk re-run re-derives attribution from scratch, so the 264 is an upper bound on the filter's share — some of it is the re-run moving cluster boundaries, which this comparison cannot separate. Whether 264 unattributed segments buys the Operator's Voiceprint is an exchange-rate question, and the exchange rate is open on purpose.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 2bb3cb1

## Q262 — ops/nickel — irreversible-action

**Question:** Q241 disabled the login item on `macbook-pro-nickel` until a build from `main` was installed. Q259 established that it is. Re-enable it?
**Options considered:** leave it disabled / `launchctl enable` only / enable and also bootstrap so the service is listed and loaded now
**Chosen:** `launchctl enable gui/501/com.evertranscript.core`, on the user's instruction. **Enabled and verified.** Not bootstrapped — that half is deferred to the next login, deliberately.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user instructed it directly, and Q241's stated condition is met. **Before:** `print-disabled gui/501` read `"com.evertranscript.core" => disabled`. **After:** it reads `=> enabled`, exit 0. The plist is unchanged at `~/Library/LaunchAgents/`, `plutil -lint` says OK, `RunAtLoad = true`, `KeepAlive = false`, and its target `/Applications/EverTranscript.app/Contents/Resources/evertranscript` exists, is executable, and is the ReDimNet2 build. The app's running state is as found: PIDs 70209 and 70215 both still alive, nothing started or stopped. **Why "listed and loaded" is not claimed.** `launchctl enable` clears the disabled override; it does not bootstrap, and `launchctl print gui/501/com.evertranscript.core` still reports no such service in the domain. Bootstrapping it now would list it and then immediately fail: `transport.rs:188-196` makes a second Core connect to the existing socket, find it live, and refuse with `AddrInUse`, so with `KeepAlive = false` the job would exit non-zero and stay down — a spurious failure recorded in launchd, and a change to running state the instruction ruled out. The agent bootstraps for free at the next login, when the app's Core is not holding the socket, and `RunAtLoad` starts it. The verifiable half was verified; the other half is a login away and needs nothing. **Correction to Q259's cited evidence.** That entry, NOTES and the host memory cited `strings … | grep -c redimnet2-b3` returning **19**; the reproducible command returns **9**, because `grep -c` counts matching *lines* and this binary's string table is a handful of enormous ones. The conclusion is unchanged and the evidence is now stronger: the installed binary embeds `dcecdce7d52bbd4739b24d0874359ec564d43f4b3a392f0104f505593b566d41` beside `diarize-embedding.onnx` and the `soulmachine/evertranscript-redimnet2-b3-vox2-lm` URL — it expects exactly the bytes on disk, so it cannot read them as `Corrupted`. NOTES and the memory now tell a reader to grep the hash, not the model name.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 2bb3cb1

## Q263 — issues/15 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q261 reported 264 newly-unattributed segments and 9 pseudonyms losing vectors as the filter's cost. What is that cost actually made of — did any clean speech lose its owner, and were those pseudonyms real voices or double-talk artefacts?
**Options considered:** accept the aggregate as the cost / classify the lost segments by channel and overlap and characterise the pseudonyms against the known-voice gallery
**Chosen:** Classified. **No clean speech lost an owner — zero.** All **265** lost segments (264 was a net; one gained) are mic audio with the system channel talking over them, 2963 s of double-talk, and **none belonged to a named Speaker or the Operator**. The pseudonym figure was also a net: **13 lost a vector, 4 were newly minted**, and the 13 were unrecognisable fragments, not voices.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **The lost segments.** Diffing owners by segment id between the untouched real History and the rebuilt copy: 265 lost an owner, 1 gained, 189 changed hands. Classifying the 265 by the same intersection test the rule uses, applied to turns: **265 of 265 are `channel=mic` with `overlapped=True`**, totalling 2962.9 s. The count of clean segments — no opposite-channel speech intersecting — that lost an owner is **0**. Their former owners are ten pseudonyms, led by `d893116fdf4e` (163), `cd5b6208b876` (61) and `d878d7818f0a` (14); segments formerly owned by a named Speaker or the Operator that lost their owner: **0**. So the currency the filter spends is double-talk mic audio that was labelled with a pseudonym, and it spends none of anyone's clean speech and none of anyone's name. **The pseudonyms.** 20 held a vector before and 11 after, but that is a net of **13 lost and 4 newly minted**, so "9 lost" in Q261 was wrong — it was the difference, not the movement. Scoring each lost vector against a gallery of the Operator's clean voice `d859b1` and all six named Voiceprints: **none of the 13 reaches `MATCH_FLOOR` (0.62) against any of them.** The two highest are `dfd6d8f20e83` at 0.5370 and `d878d7818f0a` at 0.5209, both leaning toward `Ming Chen` `7f584e` — below the floor but consistent with her far-end voice picked up on the mic, which is the double-talk artefact shape. The family named in the doubt is all present and all weak: `d893116fdf4e` tops out at 0.4332 (Jack), `cd5b6208b876` at 0.3386 (Marc), `94a7eccc7160` at 0.2402 (Hong Li). **The discriminator between a fragment and an unnamed person** is not gallery similarity — a genuinely new voice would also score low — but how much evidence the cluster ever had: **11 of the 13 held exactly one exemplar in exactly one Meeting**, and the other two held 2 and 4 across as many Meetings. A single-exemplar, single-Meeting cluster matching nothing above the floor cannot be recognised by `operator::identify`'s rule 3 in any future Meeting, so its vector was never doing work. **One honest limit.** Ten of the 13 are mic-only and align with the rule; three — `994ede0d3ec8`, `dfd6d8f20e83`, `9d7f5e39c515` — are system-channel, which the mic-only filter cannot touch by construction, and all three ended with zero exemplars. Those are re-run cluster churn, not the filter, and this comparison cannot prove it further; the 4 newly minted pseudonyms are where that evidence most likely went. **What this does to the exchange rate.** It does not settle it, which stays the user's, but it narrows it: the cost is not 6.4% of speech losing its owner, it is 6.4% of segments — all of them simultaneous-speech mic audio — losing a numbered placeholder, against the Operator gaining a working Voiceprint. The rebuilt copy was then deleted as instructed.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** d5a84bb

## Q264 — ops/nickel — gate-resolution

**Question:** The installed Core is from 15:37, before `ea13e2b`, so a re-run through it would re-mint Q248 and a real rebuild needs a newer install. How was the 15:35 install actually done, can the same artefact be staged from current `main`, and would installing it cost the microphone and system-audio grants?
**Options considered:** infer the recipe / reconstruct it from the host's own evidence and stage the artefact the same way
**Chosen:** Reconstructed and staged from `6579ed0`. **The bundle is built but NOT signed**, because codesign cannot reach the login keychain from a non-interactive ssh session. **As staged it would cost every grant; signed with the same identity it costs none.** Nothing was copied into `/Applications` and nothing was launched.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **How the 15:35 install was done, from the host's evidence.** `/tmp/et-swap.sh` is stamped 15:33:38, two minutes before the bundle, and it is the installer: it gates on the Core reporting `Idle` *inside* the script, backs the History up to `~/Library/Application Support/EverTranscript/backups/`, re-checks Idle as the statement immediately before `rm -rf`, then `ditto`s from `packaging/out/installers/mac-arm64/EverTranscript.app` and `open`s the app. `/tmp/et-sign.sh` (Sep 15) is the signing step: inside-out `codesign --force --options runtime --timestamp=none --sign "Apple Development: Frank Dai (CCDB33UUQ9)"` over dylibs, `chrome_crashpad_handler`, `ShipIt`, four frameworks, the Helper `.app`s, both sidecars and the bundle, with `packaging/macos/entitlements.plist` on the apps and sidecars. The recipe memory `local-macos-install-recipe.md` supplies the build: `corepack prepare pnpm@9.15.4 --activate`, `./packaging/build.sh`, then `CSC_IDENTITY_AUTO_DISCOVERY=false pnpm -C clients/electron package`. **The reconstruction is confirmed by mtimes**, because `ditto` preserves them: `/Applications/EverTranscript.app` reads 15:35:52 and its `Contents/Resources/evertranscript` 15:37:07 — the *source tree's* stamps, the binary re-signed after the bundle was packaged — and the source bundle was still on disk with those exact stamps and a Core byte-identical to the installed one (`2a61646eaa1c27ca`). **Preserved before building over it:** `build.sh` starts with `rm -rf out/`, and that staged bundle was `et-swap.sh`'s documented reinstall fallback, so it was `ditto`d to `~/EverTranscript-backups/EverTranscript.app.installed-20260917-153552` and verified with `codesign --verify --deep --strict` first. **Staged.** Repo fast-forwarded to `6579ed0`; the build needed mise's node (v24.21.0, pnpm 9.15.4) on `PATH`, since a non-interactive shell finds only Homebrew's node and no `corepack`. The bundle is at `packaging/out/installers/mac-arm64/EverTranscript.app`, mtime 21:49:04, Core `325e4b825ef94e6a` — a different binary from the installed `2a61646e…`, which is the point: it carries `ea13e2b`. **Signing is the user's, and not by choice.** `et-sign.sh` failed at the first dylib with `errSecInternalComponent`; the cause is `security show-keychain-info login.keychain-db` → **"User interaction is not allowed"**. The identity is present and valid (`0AE641B1114AE3BE7B2A5E4EDF608489E8B97F76`), but a non-interactive ssh session cannot unlock the keychain holding its private key, so this step is inherently attended — which matches the standing rule that the install is the user's. **What the grants are keyed on, read-only.** The user TCC database holds `kTCCServiceMicrophone`, `kTCCServiceCalendar` and `kTCCServiceSystemPolicyDocumentsFolder` for `com.evertranscript.client`, all allowed; the **system** database holds the system-audio grant as `kTCCServiceScreenCapture` for the same client, allowed, plus Accessibility. Every one is `client_type = 0`, keyed on the bundle identifier rather than a path. Decoding each stored `csreq` gives the **same** requirement for all four: `identifier "com.evertranscript.client" and anchor apple generic and certificate leaf[subject.CN] = "Apple Development: Frank Dai (CCDB33UUQ9)" and certificate 1[field.1.2.840.113635.100.6.2.1]` — identical to the installed bundle's designated requirement. The 15:37 tccd log confirms the Core's access is charged to the bundle: `AUTHREQ_ATTRIBUTION` shows responsible `com.evertranscript.client` (pid 70209) and accessing `evertranscript` (pid 70215), with `AUTHREQ_SUBJECT: subject=com.evertranscript.client`. **The answer on cost.** As staged the bundle is ad-hoc linker-signed with `Identifier=Electron`, no Team ID and a bare-cdhash requirement, so it fails all three clauses and **installing it unsigned would void microphone, system audio, calendar, documents and accessibility together**. Its `Info.plist` already carries `CFBundleIdentifier = com.evertranscript.client`, so signing with that same Apple Development identity satisfies the requirement exactly and **the grants survive with no re-prompt**. `spctl` will still say rejected — it is not notarized — which the recipe notes only matters for quarantined downloads.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** d5a84bb

## Q265 — issues/15 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q258 escalated the direction of the drop rule — symmetric as specified, or mic-only — and left `Chosen: —`. The user answered it. Which is it?
**Options considered:** keep it symmetric as specified / drop only mic windows overlapped by system speech / make it a tunable proportion
**Chosen:** **Mic-only.** A mic window is dropped when the system channel carries voiced speech intersecting it; a system window is never dropped for overlapping the mic.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user's instruction was the two words `mic-only`, sent with `build ticket 15, measure the rebuild, journal`, which answers Q258 on the reading it escalated. This entry exists because Q258 is still marked `escalated` with no resolution pointing at it, so the journal read as two answers to one question — the ticket's last acceptance criterion cited Q258 as settled while Q258 itself said nothing was adopted. **The grounds, as Q258 measured them.** The asymmetry is physical, not a threshold: the mic recording can carry far-end speech because the speakers leak into the microphone (Q246's path), while the system recording is a tap of the output stream that the Operator's voice has no route into. So only one direction can contaminate. The cost of getting it wrong was quantified before the choice: the Operator's repair is bit-for-bit identical either way, because every one of their exemplars is on mic, while the six named controls hold 1372 of 1412 exemplars on the system channel — so symmetric drops **76%** of all usable exemplars and mic-only about **2%**, for the same repair. **Vindicated by the rebuild, not just argued.** Q261 measured mic-only on a rebuilt copy: every enrolled mic window the far end talked over is gone (27 of 27 → 0 of 133), the exemplar corpus *grew* 1472 → 1776 rather than losing three quarters of itself, and Q263 showed the cost falls entirely on mic double-talk — 265 segments, zero of them clean, none owned by a named Speaker or the Operator. **What this does not change:** the rule still carries no threshold, and `Menggang Xu` is still stranded and still reported rather than accommodated — their single exemplar is on mic and overlapped, so both directions drop it. Nothing about mic-only softened the instruction that a stranded named Speaker is to be reported.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q258 — that entry escalated the direction and recorded `Chosen: —`; the user chose mic-only, and this entry carries the choice so the ticket's criterion and the journal agree
**Ref:** 0826b1c

## Q266 — issues/12 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q245 escalated the Operator's four missing Meetings: their own record holds two orthogonal voices, a merge would cement a second person under a confirmed name, and a re-enrol "has to choose which of the two groups is the Operator, and nothing in the vectors says which — that needs a person to listen to two ten-second windows." Does ticket 15 dissolve that?
**Options considered:** it dissolves entirely / it dissolves nothing and the Meetings stay parked / it closes the product call and the data question, leaving a narrower confirmation
**Chosen:** **It closes most of it.** The re-enrol happened without a merge, and the choice between the two groups is now made by a physical discriminator rather than by ear. What remains is a one-window confirmation and a standing in-room blind spot — not the two-way choice Q245 could not make. My closing line last turn, that the four Meetings "stay parked", was wrong and is withdrawn.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** **The mapping is exact, which is what makes this answerable.** Q245's two groups are `{0,2,3,6}`, matched by pseudonym `d859b11a42e1` at 0.92–0.96, and `{1,4,5,7,8}`, matched by `d893116fdf4e`, `cd5b6208b876` and `94a7eccc7160` at 0.56–0.90. Reading the real History for the four parked Meetings, `d859b1` owns **5, 10, 31 and 35** mic segments in `01a0a295`, `01a0a7b9`, `01a0a7da` and `01a0ac58` — **the exact counts Q261 records the Operator gaining in each.** So the rebuild's Operator attribution in those Meetings is precisely `d859b1`'s segments, and the other group's (`d893` 24+50, `cd5b` 61, `94a7` 10, `d878d7` 5) went **unattributed**, not merged. **Q245's product call is therefore honoured rather than bypassed:** a merge was the option it ruled out because it would cement a second person under the Operator's confirmed name, and nothing was merged — the second voice lost its label and its vector (Q263) instead of acquiring the Operator's name. **What decided "which group", and why it is not a coin flip.** These are not quiet rooms: the four Meetings carry 184, 220, 130 and 228 system-channel segments, and **87–99% of their mic segments are overlapped by far-end speech**. A voice on the mic whose every window is overlapped is consistent with leakage from the speakers; a voice with windows in the far end's silences is in the room. Group `{1,4,5,7,8}` was dropped because all of its windows were overlapped, and `d859b1`'s survived. So there *is* now something in the data that says which, where Q245 found nothing — the discriminator is "who speaks when the far end is silent", which is the ticket itself. Q263 adds weak support: the dropped group's best matches are all to Speakers who live on the system channel (`d893` 0.4332 to Jack Ahn, whose 890 exemplars are 866 on system), i.e. far-end voices, though none reaches `MATCH_FLOOR` so this is a lean, not a proof. **What remains, stated as narrowly as it really is.** (a) The claim "the mic voice that speaks in the far end's silences is the Operator" holds only if no second person was in the room. Ticket 15 cannot see an in-room second voice — the system channel is silent then, so nothing marks it — and that is the mirror of the ticket's own declared blind spot. If one of the Operator's 133 surviving windows is an in-room colleague, they are now inside the Operator's identity. (b) The verification Q245 asked for therefore shrinks rather than vanishing: from choosing between two ten-second windows to **confirming one** — play a surviving `d859b1` window and hear whether it is Frank Dai. That is still only the user's to do. (c) None of this is on the real record yet; it is measured on a copy, and Q267's re-run is what would move it. **Why I am not claiming more.** `d859b1` has been labelled "the Operator's own clean voice" throughout Q246–Q263, and that label was always a convention from Q245's grouping, never a confirmed identification. The rebuild's 0.9960 says the minted Voiceprint agrees with `d859b1`; it does not say `d859b1` is Frank Dai. The re-run makes the machine commit to that reading on the real record, which is the right outcome if the survivor is the Operator and a silent misattribution if it is not — hence (b) remains genuinely open rather than a formality.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q245 — its escalation asked a person to choose between two groups; the drop rule supplies a physical discriminator that chooses one, and the merge it ruled out did not happen. Its two-voice finding and its root-cause hypothesis stand unchanged, and the confirmation it asked for survives in the narrower form above
**Ref:** 0826b1c

## Q267 — issues/15 — irreversible-action

**Question:** Should ticket 15's filter be run against the real History?
**Options considered:** leave it measured on a copy / run it on the real History after the attended install, with a fresh backup and the Q261 measurement repeated
**Chosen:** **It happens** — `diarize/rerunRequest` on the real History after the attended sign-and-swap, with a `.backup` and `PRAGMA integrity_check` first as Q234 did, and the Q261 measurement repeated against the real record afterwards. Written up as the next session's runbook in NOTES' nickel section. No watcher armed.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user decided it, in those terms, after Q261's measurement and Q263's classification of the cost. **Why it needs the install first:** the running Core is `2a61646e…` from 15:37, which predates `ea13e2b`, so a bulk re-run through it would enrol the contaminated mic windows again and re-mint the Q248 blend — the staged bundle from `6579ed0` (`325e4b82…`) is the one that carries the filter. The sign-and-swap stays the user's, because codesign cannot reach the login keychain over ssh (Q264). **What the runbook pins,** so the next session does not re-derive it: the binary is verified by its **embedded hash and mtime, never the version string**, since both builds report `1.1.1` (Q259); the backup and its integrity check precede the request, with the pre-request `shasum` recorded so the effect is attributable; the request must be **bulk**, because `rerun::is_bulk_work` gates a per-Meeting `diarize run` out of the reseed path, and `initialize` must be first on the connection or the Core answers `-32001`. **The expected result is written down in advance, which is what makes it a check rather than a story:** Operator near 0.9960 against `d859b1` from ~139 exemplars and attributed in all 12 Meetings, `Menggang Xu` unrecognised with `forgotten = 0`, ~265 mic double-talk segments unlabelled, 13 pseudonyms losing vectors and ~4 minted, five named controls at 0.9536–1.0000. **The stop conditions are the part worth having:** restore the backup if the Operator's Voiceprint falls below `MATCH_FLOOR` against `d859b1` or matches a *named* Speaker above it (it minted somebody else); if a named Speaker other than `Menggang Xu` loses its vector or anyone gains `forgotten = 1`; if segments losing an owner run far past 265, or **any clean segment loses its owner**, which Q263 measured as exactly zero and is therefore a sharp tripwire; if any exemplar is stamped other than `redimnet2-b3`/`1` or the embedding file moves off 18 045 013 / `dcecdce7…`, meaning a re-fetch. **What this does not settle:** the exchange rate stays open — the re-run spends 265 double-talk labels to buy the Operator a Voiceprint, and the user has now chosen to spend it on the real record, which is not the same as endorsing the ratio in general. Q266's one-window confirmation also stays open and is not a precondition the user set.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 0826b1c

## Q268 — interactive/ci — gate-resolution

**Question:** Every report on 2026-09-17 says the local suite is green; none says CI. Is `origin/main` actually green on GitHub Actions, and if not, what is wrong?
**Options considered:** trust the local greens / verify each workflow and job on the pushed SHAs and fix forward whatever is red
**Chosen:** **Verified, and it was red — for 43 consecutive runs, on two independent causes, neither of which is a code regression.** Both fixed forward on `main`: `d2398c5` (clippy) and `424fee1` (the model CI fetches). The confirming run is in flight at the time of writing and its conclusion is reported separately rather than predicted here.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The user asked for it in those terms: *"Verify origin/main at 77ad5ff on GitHub Actions — every workflow, including the Windows build that et-watch-ci.sh was written to watch — and the client typecheck against the bindings regenerated since 3e56a1c."* **The scope first, because "every workflow" turned out to be a smaller set than it sounds.** There are two: `ci.yml`, which runs on every push to `main`, and `package.yml`, which triggers only `on: push: tags: ["v*"]`. So `package.yml` has not run today and is *not* expected to have — nothing was tagged. The Windows build `et-watch-ci.sh` watches is therefore not `package.yml` at all but `ci.yml`'s `Rust (windows-latest)` job, which does run on every push; a watcher pointed at the tag-gated workflow would wait forever on an ordinary push, which is worth writing down because that is what it looks like from outside. **What the history says.** The last green run is `370c471` (09-16 21:02). Every run since — 43 of them, `77ad5ff` among them at [run 35309206217](https://github.com/EverTranscript/EverTranscript/actions/runs/35309206217) — failed. **Cause one: a lint that activated under us.** Both Rust jobs failed at step 7, `Lints` (`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`). The first red commit is `286b5b4`, which touches only `DECISIONS.md` — so nothing in the code changed to cause it. `rust-toolchain.toml` floats on `channel = "stable"`, clippy moved to 0.1.98, and `chunks_exact_to_as_chunks` became a default-on lint. The offending call is inside a `#[cfg(test)]` block at `server.rs:4998`, which is why every local `cargo clippy` run all day was clean: without `--all-targets` clippy never compiles it. Fixed in `d2398c5` with `as_chunks::<4>().0.iter()`. **Cause two, which cause one was hiding.** `Lints` is step 7 of 12, so `Tests` and `Protocol bindings and schemas are up to date` were **skipped on all 43 runs**. With the lint fixed, [run 35309551873](https://github.com/EverTranscript/EverTranscript/actions/runs/35309551873) got further and failed at step 11, `Tests`, on both platforms. The logs are 403 to this account (`gh run view --log-failed` → *Must have admin rights to Repository*; per-step conclusions came from `gh api .../actions/runs/<id>/jobs` instead), so the cause was found by reproducing the runner's layout locally rather than by reading it: `ci.yml`'s Fetch step downloads WeSpeaker into `$RUNNER_TEMP/diarize-models/embedding.onnx` and verifies `3955447b…`, while `registry.rs:277` has expected ReDimNet2 `dcecdce7…` since `f9ff073`. Staged both models under that layout and ran `diarize::live`: with WeSpeaker, three tests fail with `embeds: Failed(Invalid input name: waveform)` — the graph is fed raw samples where WeSpeaker wants Kaldi fbank; with ReDimNet2, 31/31 pass. **The checksum did not catch it because the checksum was checking the model the workflow asked for, and the workflow asked for the wrong one.** `f9ff073` landed 09-17 02:35, an hour after the first red commit, so this has been latent inside the window where `Tests` never ran — the two failures are causally independent but the first is why the second went a whole day unseen. Fixed in `424fee1`: ReDimNet2's URL and hash, and the cache key's `3955447b` prefix bumped to `dcecdce7` so a warm cache cannot serve the model it replaced. Public and anonymous-fetchable, checked with a `curl -I` that resolves to `user_id=public`. **The rest of the ask, answered.** The client typecheck against the bindings regenerated since `3e56a1c` is green and has been throughout: `Electron Client` (install, typecheck, test, build) and `Ports ledger` passed on every one of the 43 red runs, and locally `cargo test -p evertranscript-protocol` followed by `git diff --exit-code` on `bindings/` and `schema/` reports no drift — the same check `ci.yml`'s step 12 makes, which has not itself run since 09-16 and is the thing the new run will confirm. **Local verification of the fix, so the push is not a guess:** `cargo fmt --all --check` clean, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, and `cargo test --workspace` under CI's `EVERTRANSCRIPT_DIARIZE_MODELS` layout gives 691 passed with one failure, `detect::macos::tests::a_real_microphone_hold_is_visible_to_the_detector`, which is this Mac's missing TCC grant and skips on a runner with no microphone at all. **What I am not claiming.** That the next run is green. Windows failed `Tests` with exit 1 where macOS gave 101, and without log access I cannot rule out a second Windows-only cause behind the same step; the model mismatch is sufficient to explain both, and the run itself is what decides it.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 4d1b2d7

## Q269 — interactive/ci — gate-resolution

Everything landed today — the ticket-15 mic-only filter (`ea13e2b`), the reseed and machine-path unit tests, the bindings regenerated since `3e56a1c` — had been verified only locally until this run.
