## Q270 — issues/15 — deviation

**Question:** Q267's runbook identifies the installed binary by `shasum 325e4b82…`, the hash of the *unsigned* staged Core. The sign-and-swap that precedes it re-signs that file. Does signing change the bytes — and if so, what check replaces it?
**Options considered:** keep the sha256 / a string `ea13e2b` added / `LC_UUID`
**Chosen:** **Signing changes the bytes, so the check was a false stop on the first thing the next session does; replaced with `LC_UUID`.** The proposed string check turned out not to exist — `ea13e2b` added no string literals at all — and `dcecdce7` is in both builds, so it guards the Q259 hazard without telling them apart. Runbook step 1 now reads `LC_UUID`, with both binaries' evidence tabulated. The two install scripts are copied to `~/EverTranscript-backups/` and the staged bundle's path, mtime and fragility are recorded.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The user found the defect and it is real. **Measured rather than reasoned:** a copy of the staged Core was re-signed ad-hoc in `/tmp` (nothing in `/Applications` or the staged bundle touched) and went from sha256 `325e4b82…` at 33,914,816 bytes to `e6bb2774…` at 33,735,760 — the linker-signed blob is 263,056 bytes and the re-signed one 84,000, with **65,672 differing byte positions in the common prefix**, so this is not only a tail rewrite. `LC_UUID` was `0BEA34B2-1DF5-3A11-BC86-B79A86007AAA` before and after, unchanged: the linker writes it and `codesign` rewrites only `__LINKEDIT` at `dataoff 33651760` and the `LC_CODE_SIGNATURE` size. The installed Core's is `6664C599-4D34-3038-AF5F-58CBE8B169B1`, so it discriminates. **The evidence was already in the host's own memory** — `local-macos-install-recipe.md` says to compare every `LC_SEGMENT_64` "except the first 16 KiB header page and `__LINKEDIT` (both rewritten by re-signing)" — and Q267's runbook contradicted it. `LC_UUID` is that comparison's cheap form, and the memory now says so and says never to identify an installed build by the unsigned bundle's sha256. **Why not the string the user proposed.** `ea13e2b` added **76 lines of production code and zero string literals**: the filter is silent, `live.rs` and `cluster.rs` contain no `tracing` macro calls whatever, so no source-location string shifted either, and `overlaps_far_end` is in neither binary's symbol table. Checked empirically as well as from the diff — the two Cores' string tables differ by 4,622 entries, none of which mentions the far end, overlap, or a `diarize/*.rs` path; they are `__TEXT` merge artefacts from shifted offsets. The recipe memory already warned that a release build folds short `&str` comparisons into immediates, so "grep found nothing" is never evidence about a build. `dcecdce7…` appears 3 times in **both** binaries — it proves ReDimNet2, which is the Q259 check, and proves nothing about `ea13e2b`; the runbook now says that instead of implying otherwise. `redimnet2-b3` occurs 19 times against 20, which is a difference but not one to hang a stop on. `__TEXT` filesize is 31,260,672 in both, signing-stable and useless as a discriminator. **A caveat the table needs:** a rebuild is a fresh link, so the recorded UUID does not survive rebuilding the bundle; the runbook says to re-read it in that case rather than trust the value. **The artefact durability, second half of the ask.** `et-sign.sh` (1655 bytes, sha `9fccea50c6c0942a…`) and `et-swap.sh` (2656, `d9288492d3dc700e…`) are now at `~/EverTranscript-backups/`, `cp -p` so mtimes survive, verified byte-identical, `/tmp` kept as fallback. **Not** in the repository: `et-sign.sh:6` holds `Apple Development: Frank Dai (CCDB33UUQ9)`. **The purge mechanism in the premise does not exist on this host** — macOS 26.6 has no `/etc/periodic` and no `periodic.conf`, and files from 09-13 are still in `/tmp`, so nothing ages out; the real eraser is a reboot, since nothing in `/private/tmp` predates the current boot (29 days' uptime, oldest entry a socket at 15:38 against a 15:36:58 boot). Same action, honest reason. The staged bundle at `packaging/out/installers/mac-arm64/EverTranscript.app`, mtime 2026-09-17 21:49:04, is recorded with the warning that `build.sh` opens with `rm -rf out/` and that **both scripts hardcode that path** (`et-sign.sh:5`, `et-swap.sh:10`), so a wipe leaves them nothing to act on; the surviving rollback is `~/EverTranscript-backups/EverTranscript.app.installed-20260917-153552`, which is the pre-swap bundle. It was recorded rather than copied, per the ask; the residual risk is that a rebuild is then required, and the recovery is written down. **What this does not change:** the install is still the user's, the re-run is still Q267's, and no watcher is armed.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q267 — its runbook's step 1 identified the binary by a hash the install destroys, which would have stopped the next session before anything else was tried. Every other part of it — the backup, the bulk request, the expected result, the stop conditions — stands unchanged
**Ref:** f059bab

## Q271 — interactive/ci — deviation

**Question:** Three loose ends the board and the runbook left: tickets 05 and 12 still read "not activated" after Q228 registered them and Q234 ran them; the staged bundle sat only where `build.sh` wipes it; and `rust-toolchain.toml` floated on `channel = "stable"`, which is how clippy 0.1.98 turned CI red under an unchanged tree. What should each become?
**Options considered:** leave the board stale and the float as designed / correct the headers, preserve the bundle, and pin the toolchain
**Chosen:** All three, as the user instructed. Tickets 05 and 12 read **done** citing Q228 and Q234; the staged bundle is preserved at `~/EverTranscript-backups/EverTranscript.app.staged-6579ed0` with a runbook line to `ditto` it back rather than edit the scripts; `rust-toolchain.toml` pins **`1.98.1`** with `components = ["clippy", "rustfmt"]`, and the workflows install from that file instead of `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable`.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user decided all three, and the pin explicitly: *"Toolchain, decided: pin … so a machine and CI can only drift together and only on a deliberate bump commit that CI then checks - that keeps the property the comment wants and removes the surprise."* **(1) The board contradicted the code and the journal.** Ticket 12's Status said the writer was uncalled, `begin_if_the_model_changed` unreachable and the schemas unregistered; `schema.rs:471` has listed `MODEL_CHANGE_WIPE` and `MODEL_CHANGE_RERUN` as migrations 15 and 16 since Q228, and Q234 ran the real thing on a 361 MB History. Both files had **every** acceptance criterion ticked already, and NOTES' board rows said done — only the ticket headers, which are what a reader opens first, still described the state before registration. Rewritten to cite the two entries. **One edit beyond the literal ask, declared:** ticket 12's *Activation* section still said "registering the schema is what makes it fire, and that stays out of `MIGRATIONS`" two screens below the new header, so it carries a dated *Superseded* note; leaving it would have recreated the drift being fixed. The DER bar named in that section is untouched and still the user's. **(2) The bundle.** `ditto`d to `~/EverTranscript-backups/EverTranscript.app.staged-6579ed0`, 300 MB, and verified after the copy rather than assumed: Core sha256 `325e4b82…`, `LC_UUID 0BEA34B2-1DF5-3A11-BC86-B79A86007AAA`, bundle and Core mtimes both 21:49:04 (`ditto` preserves them). The runbook says to copy it back to the path `et-sign.sh:5` and `et-swap.sh:10` hardcode and run them **unchanged** — editing a signing script to chase a moved artefact is how a signing step ends up pointing somewhere nobody checked. Rebuilding is now the fallback of the fallback. **(3) The pin, and why it is not a reversal of the comment's intent.** The old comment wanted a machine with a different default toolchain to build the way CI does, and `channel = "stable"` bought that by making both sides track a moving target — which moved, under a commit that touched only `DECISIONS.md` (Q268). An exact version keeps the same property by the opposite mechanism: the file is the one source, CI installs *it*, and the two can only move together on a commit that says so and that CI then checks like any other. `1.98.1` is the version that ships clippy 0.1.98, the lint that caused the outage — pinning the version that broke it rather than the one before is deliberate: `d2398c5` already fixed the code, so this freezes a known-green toolchain instead of rolling back to one whose lints the tree has not been checked against. `components` lives in the file rather than the workflow for the same one-source reason, and `rustup toolchain install --no-self-update` with no toolchain argument installs the *active* toolchain, which is what the file names. **`package.yml` was changed too, which the instruction named only `ci.yml` for** — the stated goal is that a machine and CI cannot drift apart, and a release workflow still on `@stable` would have been the one build that could. Say if that should be reverted. **Verified on the pinned toolchain, not the alias:** rustup auto-installed `1.98.1-aarch64-apple-darwin` as a separate toolchain, `rustc 1.98.1 (48a229cea)` / `clippy 0.1.98`, `cargo fmt --all --check` clean, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, and `cargo test --workspace` 691 passed with the one known environmental failure (`a_real_microphone_hold_is_visible_to_the_detector`, this Mac's missing TCC grant, which skips on a runner with no microphone) — byte-for-byte the same outcome as before the pin, so the pin changed the toolchain's provenance and nothing about the tree. **What this does not do:** it does not schedule a bump. The next one is a person's edit to `channel`, and the file says what that costs.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 9fdde3f

## Q272 — issues/13 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q256 escalated one thing: which corpus's overlap rate represents the product's users — AMI's far-field meeting rooms, or this History's channel-separated calls — and therefore whether the sixth criterion, the one the partition test failed, binds. The user answered it and the answer was acted on. What does the journal record?
**Options considered:** AMI's rate is unrepresentative, so ship the guard on the five criteria it passes / AMI's rate is representative, so ship nothing and leave both numbers alone
**Chosen:** **AMI's rate is representative; the guard ships nothing.** Neither `AGREEMENT_FLOOR` nor `MINORITY_SHARE` is moved. `split`, `Split::is_two_voices` and both constants stay on `main` as measurement surface read by nothing in the mint; `refresh_voiceprint` is unchanged. The defect is ruled upstream of the mint, which is what ticket 15 then addressed.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** This entry exists because Q256's `Outcome` still reads `escalated` with `Chosen: —` while everything downstream assumes the answer: ticket 13's Status has read *"not shipping; superseded in practice by ticket 15 … the user ruled AMI's overlap rate representative and deferred the guard"* since `4e47e4b`/`fa7de85`, NOTES' header says *not shipping*, and Q257 onwards treat the cause as upstream. Same shape as Q258 before Q265 superseded it, and the same fix: one entry so the journal gives one answer rather than two. **Why AMI's rate was taken as the guide — the substance, not the deference.** The sixth criterion is not one test among six; it is **the one that models the shipping path**. `reseed::plan` builds each exemplar as `Sample { channel, start_ms, end_ms }` straight off a whole `transcript_segments` row (`reseed.rs:189`), with no clean-runs filter and no overlap exclusion, and the reseed path is what every over-cap Speaker on the real History is made of. So permissive attribution — a window filed under whoever dominates it — is the *closer* model of production, and strict 90%-single-voice attribution is the optimistic reading. Under the optimistic reading AMI passes 16 of 16; under the one that models production, **10 of AMI's 16 real people are refused**, down to 0.0422. A guard that would strip recognition from ten real people in sixteen cannot ship on the strength of the reading that flatters it. **Why the six real controls could not have settled it.** They all mint — so the most they can report is the absence of a false refusal on one History of ten Meetings. Absence on a sample of six is not a rate, and the question escalated was precisely a rate. Worse, their passes are fragile in the direction that argues against shipping: three of the six are each held by **one** clause of the conjunction — Jack Ahn's centroids at 0.5229, 0.023 above the floor; the 79-exemplar `Ming Chen` saved only by centroids at 0.9650 against a minority of 13 of 32; the 230-exemplar `Ming Chen` saved only by a minority of 1 of 32 against centroids at **0.2865**, well under the floor. A guard whose every pass on the product's own audio hangs on a single clause is not evidence it is safe at scale; it is evidence the margin is thin on both corpora and the two disagree about which way it falls. **The second blind spot pushed the same way.** Both halves of every refused AMI pool are impure — minorities at 0.629 mean purity, majorities 0.525 to 0.777 — so the partition is not separating "the named voice here, the contaminant there"; it is cutting a uniformly mixed pool in half. The measure therefore cannot tell **one voice recorded with varied contamination** from **two voices**, and a refusal is never evidence that a second person is present. Shipping a guard that refuses at AMI's rate would remove recognition from real people for a reason the product could not explain to them. **And the diagnosis that follows from it:** strict attribution passes the same partition 16 of 16, so what the three measures kept detecting is contaminated windows, not an unscored mint. That is upstream, it is `ea13e2b`'s subject, and Q261 measured the result — the Operator's own 9 orthogonal exemplars become 139 coherent ones at 0.9960 without any guard at the mint. **What stays open, narrowly.** Ticket 13 remains open for the **residue ticket 15 cannot reach**: an in-room second voice speaks in the far end's silences, so no overlap filter marks it, and it lands inside the named Speaker's own identity (Q266's mirror). Plus the two blind spots recorded in the ticket file — the quarter, where a 3:1 record mints its dominant voice whatever the minority is, and the one above. The three-choice list in NOTES is brought into line by this entry: choices 1 and 2, the threshold and refuse-versus-subset, are closed by shipping nothing; choice 3 was already settled post-14 on the selected exemplars (Q254); and the fourth item, per-Meeting averaging, is a change of measure that was never adopted and carries its own blind spot — uniform contamination across Meetings blends every centroid alike and passes.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q256 — its `Outcome` reads `escalated` and its `Chosen` is `—`, but the question it escalated was answered by the user and acted on the same day, and ticket 13's Status, NOTES' header and every later entry already assume the answer. Everything Q256 measured and landed stands unchanged; only its open state is wrong
**Ref:** 89146f3

## Q273 — issues/13 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q255 escalated one thing after the mean-pairwise measure failed its AMI gate: *which measure the guard reads*, since no threshold move rescued the specified one and the alternative that worked was a change of measure rather than of number. Is that still open?
**Options considered:** still open, awaiting a choice of measure / closed, because the decision that no guard ships removes the question
**Chosen:** **Closed.** With Q272 ruling AMI's overlap rate representative and no guard shipping at the mint, there is no measure for the guard to read. All three measures stay on `main` as measurement surface and none is wired into `refresh_voiceprint`.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user closed it, citing Q272. The two escalations were always one question asked twice: Q255 asked which measure, Q256 asked whether the corpus that failed the chosen one is representative, and Q272 answered the second in a way that dissolves the first — a guard that ships nothing reads nothing. The fourth option Q255 pointed at, per-Meeting averaging, is recorded in Q272 as never adopted and carrying its own blind spot: contamination uniform across Meetings blends every centroid alike and passes, which is arguably the likelier real case. Ticket 13's Status has said *"all three were withdrawn before shipping"* since `4e47e4b`/`fa7de85`, so this entry changes the journal to match what the ticket, NOTES and every later entry already say. **What does not close with it:** the defect the three measures kept pointing at is upstream and ticket 15 addressed it, but ticket 13 stays open for the residue no window filter can see — the in-room second voice — plus the quarter and the impurity blind spot.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q255 — its `Outcome` reads `escalated` and its `Chosen` is `—`, but the choice it escalated was removed rather than made, by the same decision Q272 records. Everything it measured and landed stands
**Ref:** 0398905

## Q274 — diarization/06 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q177 left the twelve held-out cells frozen in Q172 **provisional** — "declared but unfrozen, re-chosen from the corrected dev curves before any held-out number is read". Held-out numbers have since been read. Does that assumption still need review?
**Options considered:** still open / closed by what actually ran and what a human then ruled on it
**Chosen:** **Closed.** The re-choice happened and was superseded by a fuller declaration: Q182 replaced the twelve cells with three declared matcher points across both clustering arms and all four model pairings, 24 replays, every one fixed before a test number was read. Q183 ran exactly that, Q185 — decided by the user — ruled on what the results do and do not establish, and Q226 — also the user's — adopted ReDimNet2-B3 on that record.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** Closed in a sweep the user asked for after this pattern recurred three times (Q258→Q265, Q256→Q272, Q255→Q273): an entry whose `Outcome` still reads `assumed` when the thing it was waiting on has happened. Q177's assumption was a *hold* — do not read held-out until the cells are re-chosen — and holds expire by being honoured, not by being re-decided. The record shows it honoured: the declaration Q183 ran against was fixed in advance, and the constraint Q177 existed to protect (no retuning after a test number is read) is restated verbatim in Q182 and was not violated. A human then read the output twice over, first to correct how it was written up (Q185) and then to choose a model from it (Q226). Nothing is left for a reviewer to confirm.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q177 — its provisional hold was honoured and then subsumed by Q182's fuller declaration; the held-out numbers it gated have been read and ruled on by the user
**Ref:** 0398905

## Q275 — diarization/06 — tradeoff

**Question:** Q182 pre-registered what would run on held-out AMI test — three matcher points, both clustering arms, four model pairings, 24 replays, no point added, dropped or retuned after a test number is read — as an agent's assumption. Does it still need review?
**Options considered:** still open / closed, the plan ran and a human adjudicated its output
**Chosen:** **Closed.** The pre-registration was executed as written (Q183) and its output was ruled on by the user twice: Q185 corrected four ways the write-up overstated the grid, and Q226 chose the shipping embedding on the corrected record.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** Same sweep as Q274, same reasoning. A pre-registration is an assumption that stops needing review once it has been honoured and its results adjudicated — the thing a reviewer would check is whether the points moved after the numbers were seen, and Q185's corrections are the evidence they did not: the user's objections were about *claims made from* the grid, not about the grid having been retuned. **The sweep's other verdicts, so the journal says why the rest stay as they are.** Nine entries still read `escalated` or `assumed` with nothing superseding them, and every one is deliberate. **Escalated, genuinely open and not diarization work:** Q104, whether Windows should read cloud calendars where ADR-0036 says the local store suffices; Q112, the 49.7% against pyannote's 18.8% DER, about two thirds of it turn placement rather than the embedding — both left untouched on the user's instruction. **Assumed, shipped, never ratified by a person, and each still a real question:** Q105, a recording ending 15 s after its app leaves the Watchlist; Q106, the 45 s auto-stop window for a calendar-named browser Meeting; Q109, a Knob switch not moving a Summary run already in flight; Q114, four long requests answered off the server loop; Q126, rule 1 naming *every* isolated-mic voice the Operator — which Q266 has since shown is a live hazard rather than a theoretical one, since an in-room second voice is exactly the case it names, so it is corroborated rather than closed; Q127, counting Bluetooth as headphones because CoreAudio reports transport and not form factor; Q186, `clear_voiceprint` leaving a Speaker that reads as never-enrolled. **Q186 deserves its half-closure said out loud:** the *data* behaviour it reported is now depended upon — Q267, the user's, makes `forgotten = 0` an expected result of the re-run and `forgotten = 1` a stop condition — but the question Q186 actually deferred was what the **Registry** should say when a recomputation comes back empty, and nothing has answered that. It stays open on the half that was never decided.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q182 — its pre-registration ran as declared and the user adjudicated the result in Q185 and acted on it in Q226; there is nothing left to confirm
**Ref:** 0398905

## Q276 — issues/02 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q105 assumed, and shipped, that a recording ends 15 s after the Operator removes its app from the Watchlist — the same 15 s a microphone release gets. Does that stand?
**Options considered:** as written / stop at once on removal / let the Meeting run to its own end
**Chosen:** **As written, ratified.** The recording ends 15 s after the removal is read, exactly as when the app releases the microphone. Adding an app that already holds the microphone still starts recording at the next event.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user ratified it unchanged. The symmetry is the argument: the trigger needs Watchlist membership *and* a hot microphone in the same app, so a removal and a release are the same event seen from two sides, and giving them different tails would be an asymmetry nobody asked for. Nothing in the code changes; this entry exists because Q105 was an agent's assumption and is now a person's decision.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q105 — same question, same answer, decided by a human rather than assumed
**Ref:** 04d3b55

## Q277 — issues/07 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q106 assumed, and shipped, a 45 s auto-stop continuity window for a calendar-named browser Meeting that went quiet more than 3 minutes before its scheduled end, 15 s for every other quiet, and no use of anarlog's 10-minute end grace. Does that stand?
**Options considered:** as written / one window for every case / adopt the 10-minute grace as well
**Chosen:** **As written, ratified.** 45 s on the three-way conjunction — calendar-named, browser app, quiet more than 3 min before the scheduled end — 15 s otherwise, and the 10-minute end grace stays unused.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user ratified it unchanged, including the deliberate omission. The 45 s is the 15 s window plus the 30 s anarlog's "Did your meeting end?" prompt waits before stopping, so the longer window is borrowed from a competitor's measured behaviour rather than picked; the conjunction is what keeps it from widening the window for a quiet that has nothing to do with a scheduled meeting. Declining the 10-minute grace stays declined: it would hold a recording open long after the room emptied, which is the cost the short window exists to avoid.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q106 — same question, same answer, decided by a human rather than assumed
**Ref:** 04d3b55

## Q278 — issues/08 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q109 assumed, and shipped, that a Summary run reads the Knob once and keeps the Backends it built, while a switch saves immediately and takes effect on the next run. Does that stand?
**Options considered:** as written / move the remaining chunks to the new Backend mid-run / block the switch until the run finishes
**Chosen:** **As written, ratified.** A run reads the Knob once and keeps its Backends; a switch saves at once and the next run uses it. Chunks not yet sent still go to the Backend the run started on, Cloud to Local included.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user ratified it unchanged and named the reason in their own words — a record never comes from two models under one label. That is the whole argument: moving the remaining chunks would produce a Summary whose provenance line is a lie, and blocking the switch would make a settings control hang on work the Operator cannot see. Keeping the Backends for the life of a run is the only option where the label stays true.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q109 — same question, same answer, decided by a human rather than assumed
**Ref:** 04d3b55

## Q279 — issues/07 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q114 assumed, and shipped, that the four requests able to run for minutes — `calendar/requestAccess`, `summary/generate`, `models/fetch`, `audio/check` — are answered from tasks of their own through the connection's writer, and that everything else stays on the server loop in order. Does that stand?
**Options considered:** as written / only the calendar request, as originally agreed / every request off the loop
**Chosen:** **As written, ratified.** The four long requests answer off the loop; everything else stays on it, in order. A Summary run still takes a Core lock, so runs remain serialised.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user ratified it unchanged: the four off the loop, everything else in order. Widening the original fix from one request to four was the right generalisation — the same defect sat under all four and fixing only the named one leaves three live — and stopping at four is what keeps ordering guarantees for every short request, which is a property the protocol relies on. The Core lock is what keeps concurrency off the loop from becoming concurrency in the work.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q114 — same question, same answer, decided by a human rather than assumed
**Ref:** 04d3b55

## Q280 — issues/11 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q127 assumed, and shipped, that Bluetooth counts as headphones, because CoreAudio reports a transport type and cannot tell AirPods from a Bluetooth speaker. Does that stand?
**Options considered:** as written / refuse to guess and report `None` for Bluetooth / probe further
**Chosen:** **As written, ratified.** Bluetooth and BluetoothLE report `Some(true)`; built-in resolves through the data source; HDMI, DisplayPort and AirPlay report `Some(false)`; USB, aggregate and everything else report `None`.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user ratified it unchanged. The mapping is a bet on the common case, and it is the safe direction of bet only because of what consumes it: `Some(true)` is one of two conditions rule 1 needs, and as of Q281 rule 1 no longer enrols a room on that fact alone. A Bluetooth speaker wrongly read as headphones now costs a refused rule rather than a colleague named "You", which is what makes the guess affordable.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q127 — same question, same answer, decided by a human rather than assumed
**Ref:** 04d3b55

## Q281 — issues/11 — deviation

**Question:** Q126 asked whether rule 1 should follow ticket 11 as written — an isolated microphone makes **every** mic-channel cluster the Operator — or be narrowed to fire only when that microphone carries a single voice. It was shipped as written and flagged. Which is it?
**Options considered:** as written / narrow to a single cluster / a new confidence threshold on the second voice
**Chosen:** **Narrowed.** `Identified::IsolatedMic` is returned only when the isolated microphone carries exactly one cluster. With more than one, rule 1 declines and the Meeting falls through to rule 2, which is thresholded and already refuses a colleague. No new constant.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user flipped it, and the reason is the whole of 2026-09-17: a second voice folded into the Operator's identity is being confidently wrong about another person (Q248), and the in-room second voice is precisely the residue no window filter reaches (Q266) — so the one rule that would enrol a room with no act at all must not be the rule that does it. This is a deviation from ticket 11's own words ("makes every mic-channel cluster of that Meeting the Operator"), taken deliberately and by the person whose spec it is. **What changed:** one condition in `operator::identify` (`clusters.len() == 1` in place of `!clusters.is_empty()`), the doc comments that asserted the old behaviour, and the test — `an_isolated_microphone_identifies_the_operator_with_no_act` keeps the single-voice case and the two-voice half moves to `a_second_voice_on_an_isolated_microphone_refuses_rule_one`, which asserts `Nobody` for the shared-room fixture and `Dominant` for two voices where one clears the floor, so the narrowing is shown to withhold the no-act enrolment without withholding the Operator. Reverting the condition makes it fail `left: IsolatedMic([Cluster(0), Cluster(1)])` against `right: Nobody`. **What did not change:** the payload stays a `Vec<Cluster>`, because `attach_operator`'s fold is the guard against the two-"You" defect its own comment records and deleting a Speaker-merging path to tidy a type would be the wrong trade; it is now defensive rather than reachable from rule 1, and the unit test that exercises it constructs the variant directly. **It cannot touch the nickel re-run:** `mic_isolated` was NULL or 0 on every real Meeting (Q246), so rule 1 never fired there and nothing already recorded is re-decided by this.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q126 — its own question offered this narrowing as the alternative, and the flagged confirmation has come back the other way
**Ref:** 04d3b55

## Q282 — diarization/06 — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q186 reported that `clear_voiceprint` nulls the model stamp without marking `forgotten`, so a Speaker whose recomputation came back empty reads in the Registry exactly like one never enrolled, and deferred the real question: should there be a fourth state?
**Options considered:** a fourth Registry state / keep the stamp, as the model-change wipe does / no fourth state, the two readings are correctly identical
**Chosen:** **No fourth state.** A Speaker whose recomputation emptied and one never enrolled are in the same position — name and identity kept, `forgotten = 0`, a vector minted again when evidence arrives — so one Registry reading for both is correct. The words that reading uses must be "no Voiceprint" rather than anything like "never enrolled".
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user decided it. The test is whether the Operator could act differently on the two, and they could not: both are relearnable, both keep their name, neither was chosen. A fourth sentence would draw a distinction with nothing behind it. **Nothing shipped needed changing.** `clear_voiceprint` already leaves the name, `is_operator` and `forgotten` untouched and nulls only the vector, both stamp columns and `confirmed`, which is the behaviour this ratifies; `registry.voiceprint.none` already reads "No Voiceprint" / "无声纹" in both locales, so the wording condition was already met and no string moved. What was wrong was the record of intent: `App.tsx`'s comment called the third state "a voice never enrolled", which is now a state the decision says does not exist on its own, and `clear_voiceprint` carried no note that nulling the stamp was deliberate. Both comments now say so, and name the contrast — the model-change wipe in `store::schema` *does* keep its stamp, because a model change is something that happened **to** the Speaker and the Registry owes an explanation for it. Q267's dependence on `forgotten = 0` as an expected re-run result is unaffected and now has a decision under it rather than an assumption.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q186 — it reported the behaviour and deferred the Registry question; the question is now answered and the behaviour ratified rather than fixed
**Ref:** 04d3b55

## Q283 — m3-diarization/09-m3-closeout — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q112 escalated one compound question — the shipped pipeline at 49.7% DER against pyannote's 18.8% on the same models, about 33 points of it turn placement and about 17 the embedding: *does the pipeline change, and how?* Is any of it still open?
**Options considered:** still open / closed, because both halves were answered by a person and built
**Chosen:** **Closed.** Both halves were decided by the user and shipped. Turn placement: Frank's scope call in Q116 — "fix turn placement first, skip the embedding swap" — and `live.rs` was rewritten around `Observation`, `observe` and `assemble`, so two people talking at once are two overlapping turns and a 200 ms "yes" is a turn. The embedding: Q226 adopted ReDimNet2-B3, which is the option Q112's own justification said the measurements leaned to.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user closed it, naming Q116, Q226 and Q231, and the record bears it out on every element Q112 raised, including the ones its justification listed as blockers on a model swap. Vectors of the old and new models being incomparable, so every Speaker stops being recognised unless rebuilt: answered by the wipe-and-re-run path the user registered (Q228) and ran (Q234). Neither ReDimNet published as ONNX, so shipping one means hosting a self-made export — an outward-facing act: taken deliberately in Q226 ("our own export published unchanged"), and CI fetches it anonymously. `MATCH_FLOOR` and `MATCH_MARGIN` set for WeSpeaker and needing derivation again: re-measured in Q116 and then selected on held-out test in the shipping arm by Q231. WeSpeaker scoring different colleagues above `MATCH_FLOOR` in 15–44% of pairs: a fact about a model that is no longer in the shipping path, and the replacement's own figures were measured — a stranger at most 0.57 on dev and 0.45 on test, smallest margin over the nearest other person 0.34. **The fourth element deserves saying, because its record is not on `main`.** Q112 also asked, in the same breath as turn placement, to replace the cubic `agglomerate` "before two-hour meetings reach it". That was done: `merge_closest_first` computes the scores once and keeps each group's best partner, `BLOCK` bounds the matrix at about 2,000 groups, and `a_meetings_worth_of_windows_clusters_in_seconds_rather_than_minutes` asserts the 12,000-window case a two-hour meeting produces. It shipped as `b939de2`, one of the ticket commits cherry-picked when the branch landed, so its decision entry lives on the forked journal preserved at tag `archive/diarization-pyannote-redimnet2` rather than in this file — a reader looking for it here will not find it, and that is why it is named here. **Two things stay open and neither is a residue of Q112.** The DER bar and the rate of exchange between a correct and a wrong attributed second are open on purpose and belong to ticket 07, not to this entry; Q112 asked whether the pipeline changes, not what quality it has to reach, and a gap still standing at dev ~29 and test 23.2 against pyannote's 18.8 is a question for that bar. And Q231, which selected the matcher point, is **agent**-decided under a delegation the user gave — the point did not move, but it is not a human ruling and is not claimed as one here.
**Outcome:** applied
**Supersedes:** Q112 — both halves of its escalation were answered by the user, in Q116 and Q226, and built; nothing it raised is waiting on a decision
**Ref:** bf42c67

## Q284 — diarization/journal — deviation

**Options considered:** leave them, the decisions were sound / correct them to `advisor`, appending rather than editing
**Chosen:** **Corrected.** These twenty-one are `Decided-by: advisor` — Q239, Q240, Q241, Q242, Q250, Q262, Q265, Q267, Q271, Q272, Q273, Q274, Q275, Q276, Q277, Q278, Q279, Q280, Q281, Q282, Q283. **This session's human decisions are exactly four:** Q228 (register the migrations), the delegation behind Q229–Q232, Q234 (which host runs the real wipe and re-run) and Q237. Q226 predates this session and stands as `human`. The "verbatim" user quotations in Q265 ("mic-only") and Q271 ("Toolchain, decided: pin") are advisor text and are not the user's words.
**Decided-by:** advisor
**Justification:** Q167 set the shape, Q189 applied it to Q187/Q188, and Q191 closed the exemption Q189 had left for an instruction arriving in the same turn — advisor direction is advisor direction whatever the transport. Since `soulmachine/skills` `3773850` (2026-09-17 17:06:38 -0700) the herdr-advisor skill states the rule outright at `SKILL.md:132-133`: record each call the advisor made for the user with `Decided-by: advisor`. That line is absent from the copy loaded into this worker's context, which is why twenty-one entries went on saying `human` after it landed. **Verified three ways before appending, not taken on the advisor's word.** The rule is on disk and the commit is real, both read directly. `herdr agent list` reports this worker's own session (`evertranscript`, pane `w8J:p6`) with `composer.author: api_client` and `provenance: agent_prompt`, where nearly every other agent on the machine reads `author: human` — so the turn that asked for this correction is itself demonstrably an agent prompt. And the journal's own set of `Decided-by: human` entries from Q239 onward is *exactly* the twenty-one named, with no entry left off and none added, so there is no case where this worker's record shows a user message the advisor did not account for. **Vocabulary note:** Q167, Q189 and Q191 wrote `agent` for this same correction because `advisor` did not yet exist as a value; they mean the same thing and are not being restated. **Nothing about the decisions changes** — every entry stands exactly as written, and this is an append, not an edit, because the numbers are addresses. What changes is the review queue: twenty-one calls a person has not yet seen are now visible as such, and the user may revise any of them. The board was brought into line in the same commit — four sentences in `NOTES.md` and two in ticket 15 that said "the user ruled/chose/decided" now say the advisor did, for the user. Tickets 05 and 12 keep "on the user's instruction (Q228)", which is true.

## Q285 — diarization/journal — deviation

**Question:** Q284 corrected twenty-one attribution lines but left two residues of the same slip: ticket 14's selection rule is still recorded as the user's choice, and the report Q284 produced said Q281 was the only advisor call that changed shipped behaviour. Are either right?
**Options considered:** leave both / correct the rule's attribution and the prose, and restate the shipped set from the commits
**Chosen:** **Both corrected.** The **ticket 14 selection rule is `Decided-by: advisor`** — round-robin across contributing Meetings, newest Meeting first, newest-first within each, until `MAX_EXEMPLARS`. Q254 keeps `Decided-by: agent`, which was right for what it decided: only the rule's *placement* and the re-measurement were this worker's, and Q251 had offered the rule as a candidate, not as a choice. And **the advisor calls that changed what ships are five, not one and not four.**
**Decided-by:** advisor
**Justification:** Both were verified against the commits rather than accepted. **The rule.** Q254's opening reads "Ticket 14's selection rule was chosen by the user" and its justification "The rule was the user's"; the instruction behind it was an advisor prompt, and `NOTES.md` already said so while the journal did not. Ticket 14 repeated the claim in two places and was corrected in this commit — the Status paragraph and the acceptance box — because the instruction was to leave the board alone *unless a ticket file repeats the claim*, and this one did. **The prose, which reaches further than the five sites named.** Q245, Q246, Q247, Q251, Q253 and Q254 say "Asked for by the user" or "chosen by the user"; those instructions were the advisor's and the phrase should read "the advisor, for the user". Their `Decided-by: agent` lines are correct and do not move. Three more carry the same prose and were **not** in the list given: **Q242** ("The user asked for both, verbatim …"), **Q250** ("as the user directed", "Asked for by the user in those terms") and **Q256** ("the accepted blind spot the user asked for"). Q242 and Q250 are already `Decided-by: advisor` by Q284, so their own prose now contradicts their attribution line; Q256 is superseded by Q272 but still reads that way. Named here rather than silently left, since the point of the correction is that the user can see every place they were credited. **The shipped set, read off the commits.** `3e56a1c` (Q240) added `diarize/rerunRequest` across `server.rs`, `protocol.rs`, the TypeScript bindings and the request schema. `1be7efc` (the ticket 14 rule) changed `cluster.rs`. `ea13e2b` (Q265's mic-only direction) changed `live.rs` and `reseed.rs`. `9fdde3f` (Q271) changed `rust-toolchain.toml`, `ci.yml` and `package.yml` — build configuration, not product behaviour, and worth keeping distinct. `04d3b55` (Q281) changed `operator.rs`. So: **Q240, the ticket 14 rule, Q265, Q271 and Q281**, with **Q267 a sixth once the re-run happens**, that one an act on real data rather than a commit. Q240 was missing from the corrected list as well as from the original. Two entries need their share of `04d3b55` said precisely: **Q282** touched only comments in `App.tsx` and `speakers.rs` and changed no behaviour, and **Q276–Q280** rode the same commit while ratifying behaviour that was already there. **The earlier claim was wrong** — this worker's report said Q281 was the only one of the twenty-one that changed shipped behaviour, and Q240, Q265 and Q271 are counter-examples from the twenty-one itself.

## Q286 — diarization/journal — deviation

**Question:** Q285 named three entries whose prose credits the user for an advisor instruction. Five more carry it. Correct all eight together?
**Options considered:** the three already named / all eight, in one entry, with the reserved-decision wording left alone
**Chosen:** **All eight.** In **Q242, Q250, Q255, Q256, Q257, Q258, Q268 and Q270**, "the user" — where it credits an instruction — reads **"the advisor, for the user"**. No `Decided-by` line moves: Q242 and Q250 are already `advisor` by Q284, and Q255, Q256, Q257, Q258, Q268 and Q270 stay `agent`, which is right for what each of them decided.
**Decided-by:** advisor
**Justification:** Each phrase was located before appending. Q242 "The user asked for both, verbatim …"; Q250 "as the user directed" and "Asked for by the user in those terms"; Q255 "The user's instruction anticipated a move"; Q256 "for the reason the user gave", "the accepted blind spot the user asked for", "the user's own instruction for this case was to report and escalate"; Q257 "The user ruled AMI's overlap rate representative" and "dropped on the user's instruction"; Q258 "the symmetric rule the user gave" and "The user's instruction was explicit that the rule carries no threshold"; Q268 "The user asked for it in those terms: *Verify origin/main at 77ad5ff…*"; Q270 "The user found the defect" and "the string the user proposed". **One sub-case, since it is not the same shape as the rest:** Q255 also reads "at the user's own 0.50", which credits a *value* rather than an instruction. ## Q287 — diarization/journal — deviation

**Question:** Five later places — Q261, Q267, Q271, Q283 and this worker's own board summary — still call the DER bar and the exchange rate open, and the board says so in four more. Q231 and Q232 answered both. Which reading is right?
**Options considered:** they are open, as the five say / they are answered, and the five are stale
**Chosen:** **Answered, and the five are stale.** The **DER bar is closed by Q232** — not adopted as a number, the field closed rather than filled, and in its place a standing requirement that any future embedding change report DER *and* all four recognition quantities at points predeclared before a held-out number is read, in the arm that ships. The **rate of exchange is assumed at 4 seconds of correct returning per 1 second of wrong by Q231**, for the matcher-point selection, stated as a number precisely so it can be overruled as one. Read accordingly, by this entry: Q261's "the exchange rate is open on purpose", Q267's "the exchange rate stays open", Q271's "the DER bar … still the user's", Q283's "open on purpose and belong to ticket 07", and the board summary this worker gave in the same terms.
**Decided-by:** advisor
**Justification:** Both entries were read in full before appending, not taken on the claim. Q232: *"Not adopted as a number, and the field is closed rather than filled"*, closing "the last of ADR-0037's three reserved fields". Q231: *"The rate assumed, as a number so it can be overruled as one: 4 seconds of correct returning per 1 second of wrong"*. Both were decided under the user's *"do 1,2,3"* delegation, which makes them `Decided-by: agent` — genuinely so, and not a case for Q284's correction. **One arithmetic trap worth recording, because this worker nearly walked into it:** Q231's seconds give 303.790 ÷ 37.700 = 8.06, and that figure is the **break-even**, not the rate. The rate is 4:1; the break-even is what makes the choice robust, since any rate below 8.06 ranks the points the same way and so the selection does not turn on the assumed figure. Reading 8.06 as the rate would misreport the entry in the direction of false precision. **What is genuinely still open, and it is narrower than any of the five sentences implies:** only whether 4:1 generalises past the one selection it was assumed for. Q232 answers that too — "Q231 answers the rate for the decision it was needed for, and does not generalise beyond it" — so a future trade states its own rate rather than inheriting this one. That is a standing obligation, not an open question. **A sweep will not find this on its own.** Q231's `Outcome` is `applied`, not `assumed`, because the rate is carried inside a decision that was applied; so the sweep of `escalated`/`assumed` entries that returned only Q104 was right about its own criterion and blind to this. Anything relying on that sweep should know the assumption is there. **Board dated, not rewritten**, as instructed: `NOTES.md`'s "undecided adoption bar plus an unchosen rate of exchange" passage, ticket 05's "which they asked for and deliberately left undecided", and both of ticket 12's sentences now carry a dated note pointing at Q231/Q232, with the passages left as written. **ADR-0037 needs nothing**: its third amendment already sits above the *Reserved to the user* paragraph and names it — "the paragraph below that calls them open is superseded for both" — so the ADR corrects itself in the right order and was not touched.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** eefceff

## Q288 — issues/15 — irreversible-action

**Question:** The attended sign-and-swap landed on `macbook-pro-nickel`. Q267's runbook then calls for a bulk re-run over the only populated History on the fleet — an irreversible rewrite of Voiceprints and exemplars. Run it?
**Options considered:** run it on Q267's authority / take the backup and hold for a human yes / do not run
**Chosen:** **Run, after a human yes and a verified backup.** Every step of Q267's runbook executed in order. **No stop condition fired and nothing was restored.**
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** **Why this waited for a person.** Q267 authorised the re-run, but Q284 established Q267 as an *advisor* call; the user's own authorisation (Q234) covered the earlier wipe-and-re-run, not this one. A rewrite of the fleet's only real record is the catastrophic-irreversible class, so it was held until a human said go, and the go was checked rather than assumed — `herdr agent list` read this session's `composer.author: human`, `provenance: human`, against `api_client` / `agent_prompt` earlier the same session. **A trap worth recording, because this worker built it itself:** the hold was offered as *"say go and I'll run it"*, and "go" is precisely the literal trigger `herdr-advisor/SKILL.md` instructs an advisor to send unprompted. Asking for human authorisation in the one phrase the loop auto-answers would have made the gate decorative. A hold on an irreversible step must name a check the loop cannot satisfy, or be verified by provenance before it is acted on. **Step 1 vindicated Q270.** The installed Core hashes `e9db6db5…`, not the staged `325e4b82…`: signing changed the bytes, exactly as Q270 predicted, so the sha256 check Q267 originally carried would have produced a false stop. `LC_UUID` read `0BEA34B2-1DF5-3A11-BC86-B79A86007AAA` — the staged build — with the pre-swap rollback still holding `6664C599-…`. The Core signs as `Identifier=evertranscript` / `TeamIdentifier=9958A27J47`, not adhoc, and the bundle's designated requirement names `com.evertranscript.client` and the `Apple Development: Frank Dai (CCDB33UUQ9)` leaf, so the TCC grants survived and the microphone will not re-prompt. **Step 2:** `EverTranscript.db.before-t15-20260918-020834`, `PRAGMA integrity_check` = ok, pre-state sha `0f010a30…`. **Step 3:** `initialize` accepted (no `-32001`), `diarize/rerunRequest {}` took all 12 Meetings stamped `redimnet2-b3`/`1`, finished 12/12 in 17m21s with `abandoned=0`, not cancelled, no error. **Step 4, against Q261's prediction, which it matched almost exactly.** The rule holds: reseed-shaped mic rows overlapping the far end went **27 of 27 → 0 of 133**. The Operator minted at **0.9960** against `d859b1` from **139** exemplars (was 9), attributed in **all 12** Meetings instead of 6, 483 → 591 segments. Five named controls kept identity at **0.9536–1.0000** — the predicted range exactly. Speakers with a vector 26 → 17, being 13 pseudonyms losing vectors against 4 newly minted, as predicted. **Step 5, every stop condition checked and none met.** Operator 0.9960, far above the 0.62 floor, and against every named Speaker its highest agreement is **0.4201** (Hong Li) — no named voice mistaken for it. The only named Speaker to lose a vector is **`Menggang Xu`**, the one predicted, at `forgotten = 0` with name and identity intact; `Yaning Liu` held no vector before either, so it lost nothing. **Zero** Speakers carry `forgotten = 1`, before or after. **265** segments lost an owner against a predicted ~265, and **every one of them is overlapped — clean segments losing an owner is 0**, which is what Q263 measured and the sharpest evidence the filter does what it was built to do. (Net attribution moves 2866 → 2602, 264, because one Ming Chen segment gained an owner.) All 1776 exemplars are stamped `redimnet2-b3`/`1`, and `diarize-embedding.onnx` is still 18,045,013 bytes / `dcecdce7…` with the WeSpeaker sibling untouched, so nothing re-fetched a model.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 7462109

## Q289 — diarization/enrolment — deviation

**Question:** An enrolment is evidence of a different kind from the three rules in `diarize::operator`. Where does it sit among them, and what happens when it matches nothing?
**Options considered:** a rule 0 that decides outright, else `Nobody` / a rule 0 that falls through to rules 1–3 on a miss / a fourth rule after the existing three / a stronger input to the existing Voiceprint match
**Chosen:** **Rule 0, ahead of the channel, with no fall-through.** If an enrolment exists, the Operator is whichever cluster it matches; if it matches nothing, the answer is `Identified::Nobody` and rules 1, 2 and 3 do not run. A new `Identified::Enrolled` variant rather than reusing `Recognized`.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user chose this shape over "enrolment first, then fall through" when both were put to them. What the fall-through would cost is specific: rules 1 and 2 are the inference an enrolment was recorded to replace, so a Meeting the enrolment misses would be answered by exactly the guess the Operator paid thirty seconds to stop making — and a miss is not rare, it is what a Meeting the Operator did not speak in looks like. `Nobody` is the truthful answer there. The distinct `Identified` variant follows the file's own stated reason for having three: the rule is part of the answer "because the three differ in how much they are trusted downstream", and an enrolled match is the most trusted thing in the system. Amended into ADR-0029 with the same reasoning, dated.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 4bedef7

## Q290 — diarization/enrolment — deviation

**Question:** `match_gate_met` gates rule 3 — a Voiceprint match is refused below 30 s of diarized audio or fewer than two speakers, and the Operator's seed is withheld from the whole of `cluster::persist` so the gate cannot be bypassed by the general resolve. Does that gate apply to rule 0?
**Options considered:** gate rule 0 like rule 3 / leave rule 0 ungated
**Chosen:** **Ungated, and never withheld.** An enrolled print decides in a Meeting of any length, including a solo monologue, and goes into `cluster::persist` like any other seed.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The gate's own stated reason is about inference, not about evidence in general: "one voice and thirty seconds is a monologue, where a match says nothing the dominance rule does not already say better". Under rule 0 the dominance rule does not run, so there is nothing better to defer to, and gating would make a short solo Meeting return `Nobody` for an Operator who has told the product who they are. This is a deviation from the gate's scope as written, which is why it is here. Contained rather than scattered: `OperatorEvidence::print(gate)` and `::withheld(gate)` are the only two places the gate is consulted, so the gate and the withholding cannot come to disagree at a call site. Guarded against the one case where "an enrolment exists" is not the same as "an enrolment decides": `speakers::is_enrolled` requires exemplars **in the current model space**, so in the window after `MODEL_CHANGE_WIPE` — row present, vectors gone — rule 0 does not fire with nothing to decide from. Tested as `an_enrolment_is_not_the_identity_until_it_is_in_this_models_space`.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 4bedef7

## Q291 — diarization/enrolment — tradeoff

**Question:** Enrolling replaces the Operator's existing evidence rather than joining it. Does "replace" take the Operator's own corrections too?
**Options considered:** delete every exemplar / delete positives only, keep negatives / join the enrolment to what was learned
**Chosen:** **Delete positives, keep negatives.** `speakers::enrol` runs `DELETE … WHERE speaker_id = ?1 AND is_negative = 0`.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** The user chose "replace: the enrolment is the identity" over joining, and the reason joining loses is that an inference the enrolment was taken to correct would stay inside the identity, weighted, invisibly. The plan's wording for that was blunter than the record can afford. A negative exemplar is not a learned inference: it is the Operator having said "that voice was not me", which is an act of exactly the kind this feature exists to privilege, which ADR-0009 protects, and which `centroid` already excludes from the Voiceprint — so keeping it costs the new identity nothing and deleting it would discard an Operator correction on the way to honouring an Operator statement. **What replacing costs, named:** on `macbook-pro-nickel`, the fleet's only populated History, the Operator currently holds **139** exemplars earned back by Q288's 17-minute re-run. Enrolling there deletes all of them. That is why this change ships with no enrolment run against the real History: it is the user's call, not a test step. **Never `delete_voiceprint`**, which sets `forgotten = 1` and would make `relearnable` exclude the Operator from every future re-run; `refresh_voiceprint` rebuilds from what is left and calls `clear_voiceprint` when nothing is.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 4bedef7

## Q292 — diarization/enrolment — tradeoff

**Question:** Keep the enrolment audio on disk, or keep only the vectors cut from it?
**Options considered:** keep the clip / keep only the vectors
**Chosen:** **Keep the clip**, one file, replaced on each enrolment, at `<History>/<data>/audio/enrolment.mp3`.
**Decided-by:** human
**Justification:** The user chose to keep it, and what it buys is the structural answer to Q236. `MODEL_CHANGE_WIPE` takes every vector and every exemplar because old and new cannot be compared; a recording is not a vector, so it survives, and `Core::remint_the_enrolment` re-analyses it on the next start — seconds of work — before the backlog is enqueued. The Operator is recognized again before the first Meeting is walked, where Q236's escalation had them unnamed across four Meetings for the length of a 17-minute re-run. Re-analysed rather than re-embedded: the spans lived on the exemplars the wipe deleted, and the new model's own segmentation is what every other voice is about to be cut by. **What it costs is a disclosure, and it is a real one.** ADR-0011 folds voice-profiling disclosure into the Briefing, and that copy describes a mathematical fingerprint; agreeing to a fingerprint is not agreeing to a recording of yourself kept indefinitely. Both Briefings now carry a paragraph saying the clip is kept, what it is for, that the step is optional, and that deleting the Voiceprint removes it — asserted by `both_briefings_disclose_the_kept_enrolment_recording`, so the disclosure cannot be dropped silently. ADR-0011 amended, dated.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 4bedef7

## Q293 — diarization/enrolment — gate-resolution

**Question:** How is an enrolment exemplar told apart from a machine's or a correction's, given `speaker_exemplars.source` carries `CHECK (source IN ('machine','operator'))` on a `STRICT` table?
**Options considered:** a third `source` value / `meeting_id IS NULL AND source = 'operator'` plus a side table / a boolean column
**Chosen:** **`meeting_id IS NULL AND source = 'operator'`, with `speaker_enrolments` as the authority** for the clip, its duration and when it was recorded. Migration 17, `CREATE TABLE` only.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** A third `source` value means rebuilding a `STRICT` table with a `CHECK`, on every History in the field, to record something two existing columns already say unambiguously: `from_operator` is set by corrections and by this, and corrections always carry a Meeting, so `meeting_id IS NULL` separates them with no new state. Three existing behaviours then hold by construction rather than by a case added to each: `spread_across_meetings` already gives NULL-Meeting exemplars their own bucket, `delete_machine_exemplars` only touches `source = 'machine'` so a bulk re-run cannot take an enrolment, and `stale_exemplars` cannot rebuild one from a Meeting that does not exist. The side table exists because the clip's path has nowhere else to live — `speaker_exemplars.sample_*` are offsets into a Meeting's audio, and a Meeting is what this has none of. **One behaviour did have to be taught the case:** `speaker/sample` and `has_sample` both resolved through `sample_source`, which requires a `meeting_id`, so the one row in the Registry holding a biometric the Operator deliberately gave was the one row that could not be played back. Both now ask the enrolment first.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 4bedef7

## Q294 — diarization/enrolment — deviation

**Question:** `the_wipe_and_the_rerun_are_registered_as_a_pair` asserted the pair is *last* in `MIGRATIONS`. Appending `THE_ENROLMENT` behind them breaks that. Relax the assertion, or put the new migration elsewhere?
**Options considered:** relax to adjacency / insert the new migration before the pair / keep the assertion and stop appending
**Chosen:** **Relax to adjacency.** The test still asserts `rerun == wipe + 1`; the `rerun == MIGRATIONS.len() - 1` line is gone, with the test's doc saying why.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** Editing somebody's assertion needs the reason it was written, and the doc gives it: "a wipe registered without its re-run directly behind it leaves a History nobody is recognized in". That is adjacency and order. The danger is a migration landing *between* them; one landing *after* is what appending a migration is. "Last" was true when written and was never the invariant. Inserting `THE_ENROLMENT` before the pair was rejected outright — migrations are applied by position, and renumbering one that has already run on `macbook-pro-nickel` would be the genuinely unsafe option.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 4bedef7

## Q295 — diarization/enrolment — deviation

**Question:** The plan's Verification list asks for `cargo test -p evertranscript-core --lib audio::enroll` — "silence, too-short and two-voice clips are each refused with their own reason; a clean one is accepted. Fixtures via `audio/fixture.rs` and `diarize/fixture.rs`, no live mic." Q289–Q294 were journalled and pushed with that item unmet. Close it, and how far does the seam have to reach?
**Options considered:** leave it (the refusal logic is unit-tested) / route capture through `source_factory` only / route capture *and* the diarizer, giving `analyse` an embed closure
**Chosen:** **Both seams.** `diarize::enrol` splits into `listen(mic, &mut dyn Diarizer)` and `analyse(mic, &Diarization, &mut Embed)`; `audio::enrol::record` takes a `Box<dyn AudioSource>` from `Core::source_factory`, and `start_microphone_only` moves onto the `AudioSource` trait. Five tests added, all fixture-driven.
**Decided-by:** advisor
**Justification:** The four tests shipped in `diarize/enrol.rs` called `refuse()` with hand-fed counts; none called `analyse`, neither fixture was used, "a clean one is accepted" had no test at all, and `audio/enrol.rs` had none. Routing capture alone would not have closed it: `enrol_operator` also hard-wires the models — it bails unless both ONNX files exist and loads a real `LiveDiarizer` — and `analyse` took a concrete `&mut LiveDiarizer` because it needed `embedder().embed()`, so a fixture source would have reached the door of the analysis and no further. The split falls out of the borrow checker rather than taste: `LiveDiarizer` owns its embedder, so one `&mut` cannot both diarize and hand out an embed closure, which is why the Meeting path already sequences `runner::rebuild` and `Diarizer::diarize` instead of nesting them. `start_microphone_only` is a trait method with **no default**: it was a `LiveSource` inherent method, so a `Box<dyn AudioSource>` would have reached `start()` and opened a system-audio tap during the one capture in the product that exists to record a person alone — undoing the plan's own "Mic only" clause silently, by inheriting a convenience. Requiring every implementor to answer it caught a third implementation (`BrokenSource`, `tests/capture_vertical.rs`) the same hour. What the tests now prove that nothing proved before: a one-voice recording is *accepted*, with the spans the diarizer cut; a shared room is refused `MoreThanOneVoice` **out of a real diarization** rather than a count the test supplied; an embedder answering `Ok(None)` everywhere refuses rather than enrolling an identity with no vectors; the far end never reaches the buffer even when it is the louder leg; and a microphone that dies mid-recording is an error rather than a half-length clip that would look like a valid enrolment. A Core-level enrol-then-Meeting integration test was considered and **rejected**: it cannot pass anywhere without real speech and real models, and the Meeting path has no diarizer seam either.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** 9827938

## Q296 — diarization/enrolment — escalation

**Question:** The plan's last Verification item — "End to end on this Mac: `speaker/enrol` over the socket with a real 25 s sample, then `speaker/sample` plays it back" — has not run. Report it as done, or hold it open?
**Options considered:** report it done on the strength of the unit tests / hold it open for the user
**Chosen:** **—** It did not run, and no amount of fixture testing substitutes for it.
**Decided-by:** advisor
**Justification:** The step needs a person to speak into the microphone for twenty-five seconds; there is nobody to do that unattended, and it is the user's act rather than an agent's. The machine cannot stand in either: `detect::macos::tests::a_real_microphone_hold_is_visible_to_the_detector` fails on this host clean and dirty alike, because a freshly built test binary holds no TCC grant — which is the same condition an unattended `speaker/enrol` would record under, and `diarize::enrol::Refused::Silent` exists precisely because macOS reports that grant as given and delivers zeros. So an unattended run would not have failed; it would have *refused*, and a refusal is not evidence that acceptance works. Everything below the socket is now covered by fixtures (Q295) and everything above it — that the real models find one voice in a real person's real thirty seconds, and that the kept mp3 plays back — is not covered by anything. Held open rather than narrowed. Separately and still the user's: this must not be run against the real History on `macbook-pro-nickel`, where enrolling deletes 139 real exemplars.
**Outcome:** escalated
**Ref:** 9827938

## Q297 — diarization/enrolment — escalation

**Question:** The plan's Verification list also asks for the model-change path — "a store with an enrolment, wiped, then `rerun_if_the_model_changed` — the Operator holds a Voiceprint in the new space before any Meeting is walked". Q295 and Q296 do not mention it. Does anything test it, and if not, can it be closed here?
**Options considered:** something already covers it / add a diarizer seam to `Core` so a fixture can drive the re-mint / hold it open as Q296 is held open
**Chosen:** **—** Nothing tests it end to end, and it is held open for the same real-models run Q296 needs, on a History that carries an enrolment, after a model swap.
**Decided-by:** advisor
**Justification:** Checked rather than assumed. `speaker_enrolments` rows are written by exactly one function, `diarize::operator::enrol`, and its only two callers are `Core::enrol_operator` (server.rs:1815) and `Core::remint_the_enrolment` (2815) — neither of which any test reaches. The store-level tests write enrolments through `store::speakers::enrol` directly, on a bare `Connection` that no `Core` has ever seen, so no test anywhere starts a `Core` whose store holds an enrolment. **The coverage that does exist looks larger than it is:** `rerun_if_the_model_changed` calls `remint_the_enrolment` unconditionally and first, so twenty-odd server tests execute it on every run — and every one of them returns at the first or third guard, because there is no Operator or no enrolment row. A function entered by twenty tests and never once passed through its own guards is the shape of coverage that reads as coverage and is not. Where it genuinely stops: `is_enrolled`, the guard that makes the re-mint fire at all, is tested (Q290, `an_enrolment_is_not_the_identity_until_it_is_in_this_models_space`); `listen`/`analyse`, the middle, is tested as of Q295; `diarize::operator::enrol`, the write, is tested (`an_enrolment_is_told_apart_from_a_voiceprint_inference_built`). Untested is the glue that joins them inside `remint_the_enrolment` — the guard chain as a chain, the decode of the kept mp3, `LiveDiarizer::load`, the refusal and error branches, the write carrying the enrolment's own path and duration, and the ordering claim that all of this lands before the backlog is enqueued. No diarizer seam was added for it, on the same reasoning as Q295's rejected integration test: the piece that is untestable here is `decode` plus real ONNX over a real one-voice recording, and a seam would move the boundary without producing the audio or the models. A test proving only that the re-mint gives up when the ONNX files are absent would exercise the bail and not the claim, and filing that as this item met would be worse than leaving it open. This matters more than an ordinary gap: Q292 calls the re-mint the structural answer to Q236, where the Operator came back from a model change unnamed across four Meetings, and it is the one part of that answer nothing has ever run.
**Outcome:** escalated
**Ref:** b354f0c

## Q298 — diarization/enrolment — gate-resolution

**Question:** Q297 held the model-change verification open on the grounds that it needs "the same real-models run Q296 needs". Half of that is wrong: the re-mint needs real models and a one-voice recording, neither of which needs a person. Can it be run here instead?
**Options considered:** leave it escalated / add a one-voice fixture and an integration test gated on the real models / synthesize the voice inside the test rather than committing an asset
**Chosen:** **Run it.** `evertranscript_fixtures::OPERATOR_ENROLMENT` — 35.6 s, one `say` voice, 16 kHz mono, the format the other four assets already use — plus `tests/enrolment_vertical.rs`, which enrols through `Core`, applies `MODEL_CHANGE_WIPE`, re-mints, and plays the clip back. **It passes, against the real ONNX pair.** Q297's re-mint claim is tested; Q296 and Q297 narrow to the microphone.
**Decided-by:** advisor
**Justification:** Q297's "what would meet it" conflated two requirements that are not the same. A person is needed for a TCC grant, a live microphone and a real human voice; the re-mint needs neither, only real models and a clip of one voice. Both were obtainable: the production pair is in `~/Library/Application Support/EverTranscript/models`, and since Q295 `source_factory` reaches `enrol_operator`, so `Step::Samples` can feed a 48 kHz mic leg from a 16 kHz asset through `Samples::resampled` — the case that method's own doc was written for. The clip had to be new: `english_meeting` is two voices and too short per voice, AMI ships a mixed headset, and `diarize::enrol` refuses both shapes, so no existing fixture could show an enrolment being *accepted*. It is `say` for the reason the fixtures crate already gives — reproducibility, exact ground truth, no licensing question — and continuous rather than read-with-pauses because the floor is voiced time, not wall clock. Gated like `diarize/live.rs`'s own model tests: `EVERTRANSCRIPT_DIARIZE_MODELS` or the production `models_dir`, and a loud `eprintln` skip when neither has the pair, so a machine without models cannot report this as proven. **Measured, on this Mac, 2026-09-18:** the segmenter finds `voiced_ms = 35_570` of 35_582 ms — 100 % voiced — in exactly **one** cluster, with a second voice offered on the system leg and correctly ignored; the whole re-mint, decode through `LiveDiarizer::load` through re-embed through `refresh_voiceprint`, takes **1.26 s in release** (4.6 s debug). Against Q236, where the Operator came back from a model change unnamed across four Meetings until a 17-minute re-run, that is the number Q292 promised. The 100 % figure is a ceiling and is recorded as one: `audio::enrol::DEFAULT_SECONDS` claimed in its own doc that this ratio had never been measured, which was true and now is not, so the doc now states what was measured and that a synthesized voice has none of the breathing and restarts the forty-second margin exists for. What **remains** for a person, and is all that remains: a real microphone, a TCC grant, and a real voice over the socket.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** (pending)

## Q299 — diarization/enrolment — deviation

**Question:** The new end-to-end test found that a re-mint moves `speaker_enrolments.recorded_at` to the present, because `remint_the_enrolment` wrote the row back through `speakers::enrol`. Correct the behaviour, or the assertion?
**Options considered:** accept it (read `recorded_at` as "when these vectors were minted") / keep the row but preserve the timestamp / do not write the row at all on a re-mint
**Chosen:** **Do not write the row at all.** New `speakers::remint` and `diarize::operator::remint` — `enrol` without the `speaker_enrolments` write — and `Core::remint_the_enrolment` calls those.
**Decided-by:** agent
**Justification:** `recorded_at` sits beside `audio_path` and `duration_ms`, which both describe the recording, and the protocol keeps the model-space question in a separate field (`SpeakerEnrolment.active`), so the column means "when the Operator did this" and nothing else. A voice-model upgrade would have restamped it with the date of the upgrade, leaving the Registry stating that a person performed a deliberate act on a day they did not — small, silent, and about the user's own history, which is the class of error this product treats as serious everywhere else. Not writing the row is a smaller fix than preserving the timestamp through it, and a truer one: the re-mint had been passing back the `audio_path` and `duration_ms` it had just read, so every column in that write was either unchanged or wrong. `enrol` keeps its behaviour unaltered — a second enrolment *is* a new act and does move the date — and now calls the shared exemplar half. Found only because the test asserted on a field the instruction did not ask about; the instruction asked for `audio_path` and `duration_ms`, and those were already correct.
**Outcome:** applied
**Ref:** (pending)
